Abstract (英文): The dominant metric for 2D human pose estimation, OKS-mAP, has three blind spots: it does not distinguish visible from occluded keypoints, does not measure joint-angle error, and is silent on the temporal stability of tracking-free per-frame inference. We propose a supplementary protocol with three modules: (M1) OKS-mAP stratified by ground-truth visibility, with Delta_occ = mAP(v=1) - mAP(full), quantifying how much the aggregate metric masks occlusion-specific differences; (M2) joint-angle error (RMSE/MAE/P95) plus knee-valgus detection consistency; (M3) frame-to-frame displacement, median-filter residual jitter, and jump rate, before and after OneEuro smoothing. We ran it on four datasets (COCO val2017, CrowdPose, OCHuman, PoseTrack18) and four models from two architecture families (YOLO11s-pose, an occlusion-domain fine-tuned variant, ViTPose-B/L), under a common detector-free oracle protocol and an open-source harness. Delta_occ is negative in every combination (-0.19 to -0.47): aggregate mAP overestimates occluded-scene performance in every tested combination. Joint-angle error is amplified in occluded layers; angle metrics reveal differences mAP cannot localize, e.g., the smaller occlusion-trained model significantly outperforming a larger transformer in occluded elbows. On PoseTrack18 val, raw jitter matched between the two YOLO models; pixel-domain OneEuro smoothing cut jump rates 9-10% at no matched-scope AP cost; residual jitter persists after detector removal; the transformer pair's jump rate is significantly lower; smoothing benefits replicate across architectures. Stratified mAP tracks full mAP closely (sign-consistency 1.0, 12/12), serving magnitude diagnosis rather than re-ranking; angle-metric difference vectors (especially knee) partially diverge, suggesting independent information. The protocol and harness are open-sourced, complementing rather than replacing OKS-mAP.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Reconstructing high-fidelity LiDAR simulation scenes from real-world driving logs is essential for cost-effective safety validation of autonomous driving systems. While simulators based on Neural Radiance Fields (NeRF) achieve realistic scene reconstruction, their large-scale adoption is limited by slow rendering speeds. Recent 3D Gaussian splatting (3DGS) approaches have improved rendering efficiency through explicit primitive representation and differentiable rasterization. However, existing 3DGS-based methods remain largely restricted to rigid vehicles and produce redundant primitives through unconstrained densification. To address these limitations, we propose URL-Sim, a unified and regulated dynamic LiDAR simulation framework. URL-Sim constructs a dynamic Gaussian scene graph that decouples the modeling of traffic participants, including vehicles, pedestrians, and cyclists, enabling accurate reconstruction of heterogeneous dynamic objects within a single framework. It further introduces a regulated Gaussian densification strategy that ranks primitives with multi-dimensional importance scores and allocates Gaussians to the most critical regions while keeping the total primitive count bounded. Experiments on the Waymo and KITTI-360 datasets demonstrate that URL-Sim achieves higher quantitative accuracy and visual quality than existing baselines under comparable primitive budgets, and accurately recovers the geometry and poses of diverse dynamic traffic participants.

</details>

Abstract (英文): A paleographer applies a script type classifier to unseen manuscripts. We measure how much testing on other pages of training manuscripts inflates accuracy. On VML-HP-ext (715 pages, 171 Hebrew manuscripts, 14 script classes) we compare page-level and manuscript-level splits. Page-level accuracy drops from 97% to 54% (59% at manuscript level), and is 39% on the official blind test set. The gap of 40 to 47 percentage points (pp) at patch, page and manuscript level is statistically significant and reproduces with ResNet18; on binarized patches (ink and background only) it is 34 to 42 pp, also significant. On unseen manuscripts the model is overconfident: mean confidence 70% against 54% correct pages; patch-level expected calibration error (ECE, mean gap between confidence and accuracy) rises from 0.065 to 0.444. Binarization leaves patch-level overconfidence but cuts it markedly at page level. Confidence still helps triage: in the more confident half of pages 67% are correct, 41% in the rest. Training on expert soft labels showed no evidence of an accuracy difference, though a small page-level loss cannot be ruled out. The predicted distributions are closer to the expert's assessment. ECE is lower at patch level (0.21 to 0.31) and in most runs at page level, but not significantly at manuscript level. Label smoothing (blurring the label, no expert input) also lowers ECE but comes no closer to the expert. Temperature scaling (post-training confidence correction) helps significantly only at patch level. On 97 pages of 16 manuscripts with mixed expert assessment, soft labels cut divergence from the expert by 22 to 30% (significant in four of five runs); the point estimate of accuracy is 16.5 to 23.7 pp below hard labels, but with 16 manuscripts the loss can neither be confirmed nor ruled out. We propose recommendations for the evaluation protocol; code will be released with the paper

</details>

Abstract (英文): Generalized category discovery (GCD) aims to discover novel categories from unlabeled data by leveraging limited labeled samples from old classes. Existing methods that rely solely on visual cues often neglect the multimodal nature of human perception, prompting recent approaches to incorporate textual information such as category tags and attributes. However, these approaches typically entangle global tags and local attributes within a shared global image representation, rendering attributes unreliable and potentially providing misleading cues that can drive the model toward incorrect predictions. To this end, we propose AptM-GCD, a novel multimodal framework that exploits apt textual guidance through attribute evidence learning and multimodal prompt reasoning. Specifically, we design an Attribute-Evidence Learning (AEL) mechanism that treats attributes as localized visual evidence rather than global category descriptors. By interacting attribute embeddings with patch-level visual tokens, AEL identifies reliable attribute-related regions and extracts discriminative local evidence, thereby reducing the impact of noisy or irrelevant attribute cues. Building upon the extracted evidence, we further develop a Multimodal Prompt Learning (MPL) mechanism to bridge local attribute semantics and global visual understanding. MPL converts fine-grained textual evidence into visual prompts and injects them into a multimodal prompt transformer, enabling attribute information to enhance category discrimination through contextual reasoning without enforcing direct global alignment between modalities. Extensive experiments on six datasets demonstrate that AptM-GCD consistently achieves SoTA performance across both fine-grained and generic benchmarks. Moreover, the proposed framework exhibits strong generalization ability, delivering consistent gains under unimodal and class-imbalanced GCD settings.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Open-vocabulary object detection (OVD) expects the model trained on base classes to recognize both base and novel classes at inference. Typical methods rely on CLIP-style vision-language embeddings, yet the learned space is often biased toward base categories, with limited effective capacity preserved for novel concepts. Such a base-dominated representation can compress novel semantics, leading to semantic collapse and mis-calibrated confidence at inference. To address this issue, we present PR-OVD, a partition-and-refine framework that explicitly reserves capacity for novel categories and strengthens category separation, while being seamlessly integrated into different detector architectures. Specifically, PR-OVD first performs coarse partitioning to build global divisions for base, novel, and an explicit background partition in both visual and textual modalities, preventing object-irrelevant embeddings from leaking into category-specific representations. It then applies category-level refinement within each partition to sharpen decision boundaries and alleviate the lack of fine-grained region-level pretraining in CLIP. To further provide reliable visual anchors for novel categories, we design a cognitively inspired Structure Prompt (SP) that encodes both egocentric attributes and allocentric context, facilitating stable partition-and-refine learning. Extensive experiments on two OVD benchmarks show that PR-OVD delivers an average 6.5\% proportional improvement on novel categories over the state-of-the-art methods. Notably, PR-OVD also benefits other open-vocabulary tasks, including segmentation and classification, further confirming its effectiveness and generality.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Surgical action-center localization requires both an accurate reference point and a decision about whether to report it. When frames with and without a reportable center are mixed, the benefit of improved localization depends on which correct candidates survive selection. We present a selective localization pipeline combining heatmap and coordinate supervision with a logistic-regression scorer. The scorer learns to identify predictions with a reportable center and a localization error within a specified tolerance. The internal evaluation uses 5,000 frames from 50 surgical sources, five source-grouped folds and two random seeds. Matched locator and scoring comparisons track correct candidates and their rejection. At an 80% acceptance quota per source and a tolerance of 0.10 times the image diagonal, our method retains 46.08 correct outputs per 100 inputs. The baseline locator with the same scoring approach retains 44.20. The gain is 1.88 (descriptive 95% source-paired bootstrap interval, 0.94 to 2.79). Under entropy-based selection, increased rejection of correct predictions offsets the higher supply of correct predictions on average. For the coordinate-supervised locator, supervised scoring reduces these rejections from 6.15 to 2.99 per 100 inputs. Evaluating retained correct outputs reveals whether a selection rule preserves or erodes an improvement in localization.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Distilling flow matching models for one-step generation generally follows two paradigms. Trajectory alignment supervises the student with the teacher's probability flow ODE, but its performance degrades under very small sampling budgets. Distribution matching directly optimizes the generated distribution, but estimating its reverse Kullback-Leibler gradient typically requires a separate network to track the evolving student score. We propose trajectory-regularized distribution distillation (TRD), which combines distribution-level and trajectory-level supervision through a momentum-mixture policy predicted in a single student forward pass. This policy serves simultaneously as a one-step generator, an estimator of the student's marginal velocity that removes the need for a second trainable network in distribution matching, and a closed-form time-dependent velocity field supervised along the teacher ODE trajectory. Experiments on SANA-1.6B and Stable Diffusion 3.5 show that TRD outperforms representative consistency and distribution matching baselines in generation quality and training efficiency for both one-step and few-step generation, with a trade-off in sample diversity.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Overlapping imaging manifestations complicate the differential diagnosis of Diffuse Cystic Lung Diseases (DCLDs), while limited annotated data constrain supervised model development. Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) can integrate imaging and clinical information, but their diagnostic reasoning remains limited by incomplete volumetric perception and insufficient grounding in disease-specific evidence. We propose DCLDs-EIR, an evidence-grounded iterative reasoning framework coupling diagnostic hypothesis refinement with multimodal evidence acquisition and verification. Multiview renderings of predicted lung and cyst segmentations characterize global cyst distributions and initialize the differential diagnosis. Competing hypotheses guide axial CT inspection to identify local findings that, together with clinical context, inform retrieval from a disease-specific knowledge graph. Accumulated patient observations and retrieved knowledge are jointly assessed for diagnostic support and refutation to revise the diagnostic state. Updated hypotheses and unresolved distinctions guide subsequent inspection, forming a feedback loop between evidence acquisition and hypothesis verification. Multicenter evaluation demonstrates that DCLDs-EIR consistently improves diagnostic accuracy over baselines and enables traceable differential diagnosis through explicit links between diagnostic hypotheses, patient observations, and disease-specific knowledge.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Multimodal (e.g. vision-language, in this work) attribution aims to interpret the models without access to ground-truth explanatory supervision. Existing multimodal attribution methods typically rely on paired modalities as proxy supervision, implicitly assuming that image-text pairs are semantically aligned. Information bottleneck (IB)-based attribution methods follow this general paradigm and instantiate it through a global sufficiency-compression objective. In realistic multimodal datasets, however, this assumption is frequently violated due to partial semantic misalignment and unreliable cross-modal correspondences. Through theoretical analysis, we show that, under such misalignment, information-bottleneck-based attribution objectives exhibit a structural limitation: by assuming equal reliability across all proxy supervision signals, they induce posterior over-contraction and forced explanations. To address this issue, we propose TIB, a sample-wise Tempered Information Bottleneck framework that adaptively modulates attribution strength according to cross-modal reliability, without assuming semantic alignment. Extensive experiments on controlled benchmarks and large-scale datasets show the effectiveness of the proposed approach, while also validating the identified failure mode.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Embodied Vision-Language Models (VLMs) are backbones for robotic planning and manipulation. However, for coordinate prediction in grounding tasks, existing architectures suffer from two distinct bottlenecks: discrete tokenization disrupts spatial continuity with high decoding latency, while standard point regression fails on multi-contact affordances and irregularly shaped targets. To resolve these challenges, we propose PhysMoE, introducing three core contributions: (1) sharing lower layers for multimodal context while routing upper layers to specialized spatial (pixel) and linguistic (text) experts by physical modality, eliminating gradient interference; (2) encoding each coordinate as a single token by reusing the visual encoder's learned positional embeddings, injecting 2D spatial priors to natively align coordinates with visual features and ease training; and (3) differentiable Signed Distance Field (SDF) supervision on pointing, accommodating affordances and irregular geometries without costly mask decoders. Compared to the base model, PhysMoE achieves a 15.7% average gain and an 8.31x throughput speedup across 9 coordinate-based grounding benchmarks (trajectory, affordance, and localization), while preserving multimodal understanding and reasoning across 11 text-based embodied benchmarks.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Discrete autoregressive models offer a scalable framework for image generation. However, they face a fundamental trade-off: long token sequences preserve representation quality but slow generation, while aggressive spatial compression reduces representation capacity. Based on product quantization, we show that, under a fixed nominal information budget, group size should scale quadratically with the per-axis spatial compression factor. Building on this principle, we propose GSTAR, a spatial--group co-scaling framework that enables \(32\times\) and even effective \(64\times\) compression while preserving strong reconstruction, understanding, and generation performance. Since larger groups introduce intra-group dependencies that must be modeled efficiently, we analyze their impact on inference latency and generation quality and introduce a semi-autoregressive decoder that combines parallel feature computation with lightweight causal modeling. With these designs, our highly compressed models perform competitively with leading models across five representative text-to-image benchmarks (GenEval, DPG-Bench, PRISM, LongText-Bench, and Qwen-Image-Bench). At \(1024\times1024\) resolution, our model delivers a \(20\times\) speedup over prior discrete autoregressive models, generating each image in 2.5 seconds on a single NVIDIA H200. Together, these results show that highly compressed discrete representations can enable high-quality autoregressive generation at low latency.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Complete-or-reject reporting of surgical action triplets can discard a correctly identified instrument and action when the target is uncertain. We introduce instance-preserving graded semantic reporting, which separates instance selection from the choice of semantic detail. Each selected instance retains its predicted mask and labels while hierarchical correctness scores guide instrument (I), instrument-verb (IV), or complete instrument-verb-target (IVT) reporting. The utility-increment rule DELTA weighs the expected gain from finer reporting against its error cost. We compare three decision rules on 5,641 test frames from eight surgical videos using fixed predictions and scores. At the 10% development calibration target, DELTA retains correct partial reports for 80.0% of candidates rejected by a matched complete-or-reject baseline (5,329/6,662). Adding IV increases source-normalized weighted correct-report yield by 19.13 to 23.13 per 100 frames across all three rules relative to separately recalibrated I/IVT controls. Weighted reporting risk changes by -0.77 to +1.01 percentage points, with complete correct-triplet counts unchanged or reduced. Graded reporting makes partial surgical predictions available as localized descriptions, with an explicit trade-off between report utility and completeness.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Ancient books preserve invaluable cultural heritage, yet their images often suffer from complex degradations caused by long-term ageing, environmental exposure, and physical damage. Existing ancient book image restoration benchmarks are limited in scale and rely on simplified degradation simulations, which cannot fully represent real-world degradation patterns and restrict the generalization ability of restoration models. To address these challenges, we introduce AncBench, a large-scale synthetic paired benchmark containing 100K aligned clean-degraded image pairs. AncBench models realistic composite degradations through a comprehensive degradation pipeline that considers material ageing, paper texture variation, blur, staining, water-induced geometric distortion, and local occlusion. Furthermore, we propose AncRestore, a degradation-aware visual-prior routing network for ancient book image restoration. AncRestore first learns degradation representations from degraded images and then dynamically selects and integrates suitable visual priors according to different degradation characteristics. Guided by these degradation cues, a dynamic routing mechanism combines expert-wise and region-wise routing strategies adaptively adjusts the contribution of these priors across different regions, enabling the model to recover both global appearance and local text details. Extensive experiments show that AncRestore effectively suppresses composite degradations while preserving text structures and enhancing restoration quality against other methods, highlighting its application potential for intelligent ancient book image restoration in cultural heritage preservation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): AAA games offer a rich testbed for evaluating agent perception, reasoning, and control. However, evaluating whether agents can complete tasks in AAA games is challenging and often human-annotated because task outcomes are often difficult to verify automatically, let alone applying it for Reinforcement Learning (RL). We introduce GTAGym, an environment and framework for evaluation and RL on GTA V tasks. Its evaluation benchmark, GTAVBench, derived from GTA V main-story missions, contains 103 online tasks reconstructed across nine task families, alongside 47 image-based shooting and sniping tasks using GTA V imagery. A web server provides access to the online environment and evaluation interface, supporting parallel testing through screenshots and keyboard-mouse actions with automatic engine-state outcome checks. We evaluate six frontier LLMs on GTAVBench with textual task instructions and on its GTAVBench-AGI branch, where instructions appear only in the first screenshot and a harness automatically compresses interaction history. The best configuration achieves 70.9% overall accuracy in GTAVBench-AGI, yet aerial navigation, evasion and interception remain difficult. GTAGym also provides a scalable generator that samples task parameters within user-specified ranges and option sets under fixed templates, creating batches of 3D and image-based shooting environments for RL with automatic reward and termination signals. LoRA RL of Qwen3.8-27B on generated on-foot navigation tasks improves the in-domain performance and transfers to out-of-domain task categories, and native GTA V tasks from 15% to 23%.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Precise multimodal retinal image rigid registration (MRIRG) is essential for aligning cross-modal vessel structures, offering critical guidance for ophthalmic surgery. Despite its significance, the advancement of MRIRG is hindered by the lack of publicly available multimodal retinal image rigid registration datasets spanning various ophthalmic surgery phases (e.g., preoperative, intraoperative, and postoperative). To address this issue, we introduce the MICRO dataset, a large-scale MRIRG benchmark, comprising 1,365 cross-modal retinal image pairs with standardized annotations across all ophthalmic surgery phases. Moreover, existing deep registration methods achieve competitive MRIRG results via a two-step segmentation-then-detection framework, but segmentation quality heavily constrains their performance. We argue that this dependency stems from an implicit need for keypoint topology constraints, which have not been exploited for MRIRG through a one-step registration framework. Inspired by the above analysis, we propose a novel One-step Frequency-Structure Similarity Registration (OFS2Reg) learning framework for MRIRG that leverages the potential of cross-modal vessel structure similarities to construct keypoint topology constraints under a shared detection network. To be specific, OFS2Reg applies a well-designed frequency-guided structure preservation descriptor to construct coarse keypoint topology constraints in descriptors by highlighting significant vessel structures. Additionally, we develop a new cross-modal vessel structure similarity learning method that refines cross-modal keypoint topology constraints to precisely align pixel-level cross-modal vessel structures by leveraging cross-modal vessel structure similarity priors.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Although remote sensing image arbitrary-scale super-resolution (RSI-ASSR) methods support reconstruction at arbitrary or continuous scales, they predominantly condition on the relative scale factor and have not sufficiently investigated the absolute spatial resolution itself as a reconstruction prior, thereby disregarding the inherent spatial context of objects, which frequently leads to spatial-related distortions and compromised performance in downstream remote sensing applications. To address these challenges, we introduce SpatialSR, a SRe-centric arbitrary-scale RSISR method that leverages the spatial resolution (SRe) of remote sensing images (RSI) as an essential prior for super-resolution reconstruction. Distinct from existing SR methods, SpatialSR aims to restore high-resolution remote sensing images with a target SRe (typically set to 0.1m/pixel to align with the metadata of commonly used downstream task datasets). Specifically, we design a Spatial Resolution Estimation Module (SREM) to estimate the SRe of low-resolution (LR) images, thereby dynamically determining the required arbitrary scaling factor. Next, we employ a pretrained SRe-to-image diffusion model to extract SRe-specified texture-style codes, which are further incorporated into a carefully designed Resolution-aware Style Consistency Module (RSCM) to constrain the SRe-aware texture consistency of reconstructed images. Furthermore, to overcome the high computational expense inherent in diffusion-based SR methods, we wrap the iterative diffusion process as a Trajectory Consistency (TC) loss, which enables comparable reconstructed texture quality of the generative model in SpatialSR within a single step. Extensive experiments demonstrate that the proposed SpatialSR achieves state-of-the-art (SOTA) performance on commonly used RSISR and downstream (image classification and segmentation tasks) datasets.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Recent work on latent-free diffusion transformers has demonstrated the feasibility of training image generators without a latent VAE. This avoids compression artifacts from the VAE and simplifies the training pipeline. In particular, JiT showed that even a standard transformer can be trained to generate images when the model is trained to predict the final image ($x$-prediction). Even though JiT unlocks training efficiency comparable to latent models, it still suffers from reduced image quality likely arising from modeling the image as a collection of large patches. In this work, we revisit JiT and show that adding simple convolutional pixel-pathway layers in a post-training phase significantly improves generation quality. Moreover, we show that the added pixel-pathway does not need to be active in the early denoising stages, and can be activated as late as in the last $20\%$ of the denoising process. Our experiments on ImageNet@256 show improvements compared to the original JiT results, achieving an FID of $1.78$ (from $2.34$ with corrected sampling), without using additional losses.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) are typically evaluated by whether they produce correct answers, but correctness alone does not reveal whether their outputs are supported by the right visual evidence. We introduce VIGOR, a benchmark for evaluating VIsually GrOunded Rationales in MLLMs. VIGOR represents visual rationales as explicit concepts paired with pixel-level groundings, covering multiple abstraction levels including low-level visual properties, intermediate structures, objects, parts and high-level motion, relations. To construct VIGOR at scale, we develop an automatic annotation framework that reuses existing dense supervision when available and generates missing concept groundings through proposal-based annotation followed by conservative multimodal verification. Using VIGOR, we evaluate whether MLLMs "look at what they say" by comparing model-derived visual evidence for each expressed concept against annotated ground-truth rationales. Our results show that current MLLMs exhibit limited visual rationale correctness and reveal distinct grounding failures across concept levels. VIGOR provides a benchmark and scalable data construction pipeline for studying whether MLLM outputs are supported by appropriate visual evidence. Code is available at https://github.com/deep-real/VIGOR

</details>

Abstract (英文): Text-to-image diffusion models can render a photorealistic analog clock but cannot control the time it displays. Across four pretrained models, fewer than 1% of generated clocks place both hands within 6 degrees of the requested time; the models instead collapse onto visually dominant configurations such as the canonical 10:10 pose. The same failure appears when a specific fraction of a circular object is requested, where models return complete or conventionally sliced objects. We observe that these two failures share a geometric structure: both target quantities can be parameterized by angular variables.
  We introduce a geometric token encoding framework that conditions generation directly on these angles. Each angular component is encoded as a periodic sine-cosine feature and projected into a role-specific token embedding that is injected into the prompt before a frozen text encoder. To spatially ground these tokens without requiring localization input at inference, we introduce Stochastic Localization Relaxation (SLR), which stochastically relaxes attention supervision from component-level to object-level to unconstrained localization during training. For clocks, we additionally introduce a hand-swap preference objective that discourages assigning the correct angles to the wrong hand roles. On analog clocks our method reaches 63-78% Acc@6 degrees across evaluation methods, compared with below 1% for the baselines. On circular-fraction generation, it achieves 89.5% Acc@0.10, while maintaining 88.8% on unseen object categories and 84.9% on unseen target values, compared with roughly 20% for pretrained baselines.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Graphical user interface (GUI) grounding maps a screenshot and a natural-language instruction to the screen location where an agent should act. Zoomed views can reveal small targets but may lose the context needed to identify them. We introduce GroundCascade, a training-free controller that uses agreement between a grounder's full-screen and zoomed predictions to decide when to consult a complementary model. It selects between zoomed predictions while preserving corroborated full-screen locations. An optional Finalizer predicts from an unmarked joint crop when disagreement remains (Full); omitting it yields the two-stage Selective variant. GroundCascade reaches 85.33% on ScreenSpot-Pro with Indeed-UI-32B/8B/32B and 88.43% on the visible-target OSWorld-G-Refined subset with Qwen3.6-27B/3.8-27B/3.8-27B, gains of 2.66 and 2.35 percentage points over the stronger standalone two-pass grounder. On UI-Vision, GroundCascade reaches 72.20% category-balanced accuracy, with Selective slightly higher at 72.28%. Across these three evaluations, GroundCascade in Full mode uses 2.42-2.73 calls per request, 31-39% fewer than unconditionally acquiring both models' two-pass predictions. At matched second-model call budgets, selective acquisition gains 1.52-2.56 points over standalone Two-pass, compared with 0.22-0.69 points from stratified random acquisition. GroundCascade leads the official ScreenSpot-Pro leaderboard and exceeds reported GUI-specialist comparisons on OSWorld-G-Refined and UI-Vision, subject to differences in external evaluation protocols.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Generative pipelines can now turn every product page of an e-commerce catalog into a short video ad, but deciding whether such an ad is usable is not one good-or-bad call. We describe how we operationalized the review and editing of these ads so that both can keep pace with generation: reviewers go through each ad scene by scene and answer six questions, on the product, scene quality, pronunciation, script, sound and captions; judges built from vision-language, speech and language models file each finding under the matching question, and reviewers accept or reject each finding and add what the judges missed. Each confirmed issue then opens an edit tool matched to it. The questions and the routing from a finding to its fix do not depend on the models that generate the ad. Across 120 ads from our pipeline, reviewed with judge findings on screen, reviewers marked the product wrong on 60% of scenes, and script, caption or sound problems on under 10% each. On 60 of these ads, they marked the product wrong on 58% of scenes, far more than the 26% marked by two reviewers who later saw the ads with the judges hidden; one reviewer who saw the ads both ways marked 57% with judge findings and 19% without. On 120 blind-labeled scenes, a product judge that watches the clip with the catalog photos reached AUC 0.84, while video-quality and photo-similarity scores were close to chance. We tune the judges for recall: the product judge catches nearly all defects but also flags many clean scenes, so reviewers decide; together, the judges widen what reviewers mark and reviewers add what the judges miss. Without judge findings, one reviewer's marking faded over a session. We release a review-portal template and our review data (120 ads and 562 scenes with judge findings and human marks, plus 120 blind-labeled scenes with baseline scores) at https://github.com/anoubhav/ad-review-and-edit.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Image geolocalization predicts the location at which a photo was taken. Accurate geolocalization requires discriminating between both physically and semantically adjacent areas on Earth. However, prior diffusion-based approaches predict locations by generating coordinates, which are semantically unstable over space. We avoid this limitation by learning geolocalization behavior in the pretrained representation spaces of location encoders, which provide both positional and semantic understanding. To make this space usable for coordinate prediction, we introduce a Location Autoencoder (LAE), which pairs a frozen location encoder with a learned decoder mapping embeddings back to coordinates with kilometer-level accuracy worldwide. In the LAE space, we train DIG, a latent diffusion model that transports noise to location embeddings conditioned on image features. DIG consistently outperforms coordinate-based generative models and sets a new state of the art for parametric geolocalization on three benchmarks: OSV-5M, Im2GPS3k, and YFCC4k. Notably, DIG is the first generative model to outperform retrieval-based methods, and further improves retrieve-and-rerank systems when used as a prior. Code and models will be released.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Background: Geographic atrophy (GA) is an advanced form of age-related macular degeneration (AMD). It causes irreversible vision loss due to progressive degeneration of the retinal pigment epithelium (RPE) and photoreceptors. Accurate segmentation of GA in en-face image is crucial for early diagnosis, monitoring disease progression, and developing personalized treatment strategies. However, existing deep learning approaches are typically evaluated using single architectures and single-device datasets, limiting insight into relative performance and cross-device robustness. Methods: We trained and systematically compared five deep learning architectures for GA segmentation: Nested U-Net, ResU-Net, nnUnet, Swin-Unet and MedSegDiff model, together with a thresholding based classical baseline. The models were trained on a dataset of en-face OCT images derived from 269 OCT volumes from two different devices, following a three-step preprocessing technique involving normalization and inpainting to enhance the quality of the en-face images. Results: On the fixed held-out validation set comprising OCT scans from both Cirrus (R) (Carl Zeiss Meditec, Inc., USA) and Spectralis (R) (Heidelberg Engineering, Germany) devices, nnUnet achieved the highest mean Dice similarity coefficient (DSC) of 0.8882 +/- 0.0033. The other models yielded the following Dice scores: Nested U-Net (0.7932 +/- 0.0423), ResU-Net (0.8453 +/- 0.0074), Swin-Unet (0.6886 +/- 0.0294), and Diffusion model (0.7959 +/- 0.0242). Conclusions: nnUnet framework, demonstrated most reliable performance for GA segmentation in en-face OCT under limited data conditions. It offers the best balance of accuracy, stability, and efficiency for GA quantification.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate and timely identification of road closures is critical for large-scale navigation and logistics systems, yet remains challenging due to the need to jointly reason over visual evidence, spatial context, and road network topology. Existing approaches either rely on manual map updates or focus on detecting closure-related objects without resolving which road segment is affected. In this work, we introduce a unified multimodal and multitask framework that jointly performs road closure classification (no, partial, complete) and road segment association within a single learning architecture. Our model integrates graph-structured map data, satellite imagery augmented with projected street-level detections, and street-view images using modality-specific encoders and cross-modal transformer attention, enabling explicit reasoning over visual cues and network topology. Evaluated on nearly 20K real-world scenarios collected from a large-scale commercial vehicle fleet, our approach achieves 72.7% closure classification accuracy and 60% road segment association accuracy, outperforming frontier vision-language models by over 28 percentage points on closure classification while being over 4,000x smaller and 100x faster at inference. Extensive ablations demonstrate the importance of cross-modal attention, edge-aware pooling, and graph message passing for accurate geospatial reasoning. This work establishes the first benchmark for joint road closure classification and segment-level attribution, and shows that task-specific architectural inductive biases substantially outperform general-purpose multimodal models for structured geospatial understanding.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Thread density, the threads per inch along and across a woven fabric, is a basic specification that buyers check whenever fabric is sold. Without laboratory instruments, inspectors count it slowly, by eye, through a pick glass, a folding 10$\times$ magnifier with a one-inch window. A smartphone photo through that glass could make the check faster and leave a record. We judge such a photo by what it is used for, as in task-based image quality: it is good if the thread count from it is within $\pm$5% of the true count, a common tolerance in fabric specifications. We introduce PickGlass-TPI: 76 factory scenes of 44 fabric patches, each counted in an open annotation tool by a textile-trained annotator, whose counts are the reference. On it we benchmark frontier and open vision-language models, CNNs, counting specialists and a training-free spectral counter (PG-Lattice). No method agrees with the reference within the tolerance. The best methods are off by about a quarter: a small open model that we tuned only on synthetic images (Molmo2-4B, 25%) is level with the best frontier model, GPT-6.1-sol (27% when asked directly, 25% given four magnified close-ups of the window). Both err less than a non-expert annotator (37%), but only the tuned model also beats, on both error measures, a baseline that guesses each scene's density as the average of the others. PG-Lattice most often repeats its count within 5% across two photos of the same patch, but its misses, like GPT-6.1-sol's errors, are often a factor of two. Even the reference repeats a count across such re-shoots only to a median 9%, so one photo cannot yet be shown to meet $\pm$5%; the image quality that matters is reliability across re-shoots. Generic no-reference quality scores chosen before labeling do not rank PG-Lattice's error, and generative enhancement raises perceptual scores but does not reliably improve the count.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Children's earliest years are a period of intense social learning, during which meaning is built through gaze, gesture, and physical contact. Yet, this critical aspect of social interaction remains under-evaluated in multimodal video understanding, as existing benchmarks prioritize the conversation-driven interactions of adults. We address this gap with \bench ($\textbf{N}$aturalistic $\textbf{E}$arly-childhood $\textbf{S}$ocial in$\textbf{T}$eractions), the first child-centric benchmark for nonverbal social reasoning. \bench comprises \totalvids manually annotated clips ($\sim$12s) curated for diversity across naturalistic settings, cultural contexts, and developmental stages. Beyond a lack of data, targeted model improvement in this domain is hindered by coarse evaluation schemas that conflate basic perception with higher-order reasoning. To resolve this, \bench introduces a compositional schema that builds atomic interaction cues into progressive levels of social reasoning: contextual, behavioral, interpersonal, and open-ended descriptions. Extensive analysis of state-of-the-art Vision-Language Models (VLMs) reveals a stark performance collapse: despite strong contextual recognition, models exhibit substantial gaps in decoding precise behavioral and interpersonal cues. We trace these failures to specific biases, including poor consequence grounding and temporal sensitivity. Finally, we ground \bench in real-world applications by evaluating VLMs using domain adaptation strategies. Ultimately, \bench provides a rigorous testbed for advancing socially grounded artificial intelligence in early childhood. Project page: https://laubravo.github.io/nest_website/.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Automated video capsule endoscopy (VCE) classification remains challenging due to severe class imbalance, limited source-video diversity, and variations in image appearance across datasets. Although deep learning models have demonstrated promising performance on established benchmarks, their reliability under different validation protocols and external dataset shift remains insufficiently understood. In this work, we introduce DMSNet, a deformable adaptive multi-scale edge-guided attention network integrating adaptive multi-scale feature fusion, deformable lesion refinement, edge-guided attention, and sparse feature selection. We benchmark DMSNet against 18 representative convolutional, transformer-based, and pretrained vision architectures on Kvasir-Capsule under random frame-level and source-video-disjoint validation protocols. We further identify source-video overlap between the original training and test splits and evaluate model performance on both the complete test set and a source-video-disjoint subset. Under random frame-level validation, DMSNet achieves an MCC of 0.442 $\pm$ 0.020 on complete test set and 0.456 $\pm$ 0.037 on the source-video-disjoint subset. On the external CV2024 dataset, ConvNeXt-Tiny achieves the highest MCC of 0.207, while performance degrades substantially across architectures relative to in-domain evaluation. We further investigate source-video diversity using a per-class cap of 200 training frames. With the total training-set size held fixed, increasing the maximum source-video coverage from four to eight improves MCC for 17 of 19 evaluated architectures. Overall, these findings demonstrate that model rankings and classification performance depend substantially on evaluation protocol, training-source diversity, and cross-dataset shift, emphasizing the need for source-aware validation and rigorous external evaluation of VCE classification systems.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Temporal action segmentation (TAS) is commonly trained on large banks of pre-extracted frame features, but storing and replaying those features becomes a practical bottleneck for long-form video, especially in incremental TAS where past-task data must remain available to mitigate catastrophic forgetting. Recent TAS replay and condensation methods reduce this burden, but they rely on dense frame-wise annotations and allocate storage through fixed segment-level structures, limiting their flexibility for long procedural videos whose frame complexity varies substantially over time. We introduce Adaptive Residual-Quantized Variational AutoEncoder (ARQ-VAE), a compact and label-free memory representation for TAS features that learns a discrete residual quantization of frame-level video features and then adaptively truncates the residual chain on a per-frame basis, retaining only the residual prefix that best reconstructs each frame feature. This yields a practical compact feature memory that does not depend on frame-wise labels and can therefore support multiple TAS settings with the same stored representation, while a lightweight fine-tuning stage further improves reconstruction quality for the truncated codes that are actually retained. Across standard TAS benchmarks, ARQ-VAE achieves a substantially improved storage-performance trade-off over prior replay and condensation baselines. On Breakfast, our final representation reduces training-set storage from 28 GB for the original I3D feature bank to 10 MB while retaining strong downstream segmentation performance; with ASFormer, it achieves 71.5 Edit and 51.2 F1@50 in the supervised setting. We further show that the same label-free memory remains effective for incremental and unsupervised TAS. We position ARQ-VAE as a practical memory representation for TAS and a promising direction for broader dataset condensation applications.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Standard 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) pipelines struggle with severely underexposed images, producing unstable colors, floating artifacts, and incomplete reconstructions in dark regions. To better account for noise in these low-light observations, RAW images offer a natural basis for noise-aware reconstruction by preserving linear sensor measurements and providing access to noise metadata. We present RetinexGS, a RAW-domain 3DGS framework that combines Retinex-inspired appearance modeling with sensor-informed optimization for low-light novel-view synthesis. We factorize each Gaussian's color into a view-independent base color and a shared, position- and view-dependent modulation field, regularizing the two components separately to constrain appearance learning from noisy observations. We use DNG noise metadata to weight the reconstruction loss for signal-dependent noise, while distortion-aware rendering avoids spatially resampling RAW targets. To address sparse geometry in dark regions, we introduce depth-guided initialization. We evaluate RetinexGS on three public RawNeRF scenes and two custom-captured low-light RAW scenes. With all methods trained on the same RAW inputs for each scene, RetinexGS outperforms LE3D and RawNeRF in mean sRGB PSNR by 2.18 dB and 2.71 dB respectively, across the five scenes, while supporting real-time novel-view rendering.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The rapid advancement of foundation models has transformed computer vision, yet their adaptation to medical imaging, particularly purpose built foundation models for spatio temporal echocardiography videos, remains limited. In this work, we explore the feasibility of fine tuning a pretrained foundation model for video (TimeSformer) for automated diagnosis of reduced ejection fraction heart failure (HFrEF) from apical four chamber videos. The model is finetuned on the publicly available RVENet A4C dataset with a phase aware clip generation strategy that captures complementary systolic and diastolic dynamics. We employ divided space time attention, layer wise learning rate decay, and multi clip inference to enhance robustness under limited data. Experimental results show that full fine tuning of the proposed model outperforms classifier only tuning and a 3D CNN baseline. These findings highlight the potential of leveraging foundation video models for echocardiography based heart failure diagnosis and motivates further domain adaptation studies in medical imaging.

Abstract (英文): Diffusion models are powerful generative methods for learning and sampling from complex data distributions. Safely deploying these models requires detecting inputs that fall outside their training distribution. Because diffusion models recover clean samples from noisy inputs, reconstruction error is often used for out-of-distribution (OOD) detection. This approach assumes that in-distribution (ID) inputs are reconstructed more accurately than OOD inputs. However, OOD inputs may be reconstructed equally well or even better, making this score unreliable. We therefore propose the leading sensitivity spectrum of intermediate denoising features as a complementary OOD signal and demonstrate that it remains informative when reconstruction error is misleading. We introduce DUal-readout of diffusion models for OOD detection (DUO), a fully unsupervised, training-free OOD detector that combines reconstruction error and feature sensitivity. DUO improves average AUROC by approximately 6% points over the strongest baselines while substantially reducing inference cost, and achieves SOTA on near-OOD and high-resolution benchmarks.

</details>

Abstract (英文): We studied domain shift in adult elbow fracture classification from radiographs when training on a large public dataset and deploying on a small, institution-specific dataset (UofL). We implemented a domain-adversarial neural network (DANN) to align feature distributions across source (MURA) and target (UofL) domains while optimizing fracture classification. A pretrained convolutional backbone was adapted to grayscale inputs; the task head predicts fracture presence, and a gradient-reversal-based domain head encourages domain-invariant features via adversarial learning. We compared four settings: (1) Source-only, (2) Target-only (limited labels), (3) Naive merge (pooled training), and (4) DANN (MURA to UofL). Evaluation on UofL test data uses AUC-ROC, and accuracy. DANN outperformed Source-only and Naive merge baselines by reducing domain bias without overfitting to the small target set. Ablations showed that progressive adversarial weight, dataset-size-aware domain loss, and domain-balanced sampling improved stability and target performance. Results demonstrated that domain alignment is a practical path to robust clinical generalization from public to local data.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Temporal Sentence Grounding (TSG) aims to localize temporal segments in untrimmed videos that correspond to natural language queries, a critical task for video understanding with applications in video retrieval, summarization, and human-computer interaction. Despite significant progress driven by transformer-based models, existing TSG methods suffer from two key limitations: quantization errors due to temporal downsampling, which misalign segment boundaries, and suboptimal ranking of predictions based on semantic matching rather than localization precision. To address these challenges, we propose the Adaptive Boundary Refinement and Ranking (ABRR) framework, a novel, model-agnostic post-processing approach that enhances the performance of any TSG model without retraining. ABRR integrates three innovative components: (1) Temporal Distribution Smoothing, which mitigates noise in predicted score distributions using Gaussian kernel convolution; (2) Boundary Refinement via Optimal Transport, which leverages the 2-Wasserstein distance and Sinkhorn iterations to align coarse segment boundaries with true temporal moments, grounded in Monge-Kantorovich optimal transport theory; and (3) Quality-Driven Re-Ranking, which reorders predictions using a composite score balancing localization precision and semantic relevance. By correcting quantization errors and prioritizing accurately localized segments, ABRR achieves substantial improvements over state-of-the-art TSG models. Extensive experiments on benchmark datasets, including Charades-STA, ActivityNet Captions, and QVHighlights, demonstrate consistent gains at stringent IoU thresholds (e.g., IoU=0.7), alongside enhanced ranking performance. The framework's computational efficiency and compatibility with existing models make it a versatile and impactful solution for real-world TSG applications.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate localization of abnormalities in three-dimensional (3D) CT scans is essential for linking automated predictions with clinically relevant pathological findings. We propose Uni-Loc, a vision-language framework that unifies pathology classification and 3D localization using scan-level supervision. Uni-Loc derives both predictions and localization maps from spatial similarities between pathology-conditioned queries and image features. A token-to-voxel upsampling module enables high-resolution localization, while gradient-guided thresholding identifies pathology regions without additional learned parameters. Evaluated on five localization tasks comprising 355 annotated CT scans, Uni-Loc achieves macro-averaged voxel-level AUROC/AUPR of 96.1\%/34.5\%, outperforming relevance propagation (80.7%/13.9%) and gradient saliency (88.7%/8.1%). Uni-Loc also achieves classification AUROC of 88.39% on CT-RATE and 78.03% on RAD-ChestCT, improving over a matched baseline. These results demonstrate effective 3D pathology localization without requiring pathology-specific voxel-level supervision.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Hyperbolic vision-language models (VLMs) have emerged as a rapidly growing area of research, reporting strong zero-shot performance alongside an interpretable embedding space in which radius orders representations by generality. But how hyperbolic are hyperbolic VLMs in practice? We investigate this at the exponential map, the single operation through which curvature enters an otherwise Euclidean network. We show that across MERU, HyCoCLIP, ARGENT, and PHyCLIP, the exponential map is well approximated by the identity function, i.e., pairwise distances and angles in the pre-map Euclidean space barely change under projection, and removing the map at inference leaves evaluation scores unchanged. Motivated by this observation, we train Euclidean VLMs on CC3M from scratch under the same hierarchy losses. Over roughly the final 90% of the training steps, the training statistics are nearly indistinguishable from those of their hyperbolic counterparts, and the resulting models match the evaluation scores and recover the same radial distributions. Our findings establish Euclidean space as a sufficient substrate for hierarchical vision-language learning as it is currently practiced, and motivate new paradigms in which hyperbolic geometry can deliver on its theoretical promise. Code is available at https://github.com/fbyrman/hyperbolic-vlms.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Automated segmentation in multi-view echocardiography is essential for comprehensive cardiac assessment, yet remains challenging due to substantial anatomical and appearance variability across standard acoustic windows. In this paper, we present P2Echo, a unified framework capable of segmenting diverse cardiac structures across multiple standard echocardiographic views using a single model controlled by structured view-aware text prompts. Unlike recent foundation model adaptations that rely on implicit latent priors, P2Echo explicitly conditions the segmentation process via a novel Dynamic Image-Text Alignment (DyITA) module. DyITA integrates a differentiable routing mechanism and token differential operators to dynamically modulate decoder features according to the requested view and target structure. This allows the network to adaptively switch its processing pathways for different views without architectural changes. Extensive experiments on a large-scale internal dataset (seen during training) and held-out evaluation on three external datasets demonstrate that P2Echo matches or exceeds both view-specific specialists and state-of-the-art universal models at a fraction of their computational cost, offering an efficient, controllable solution for multi-plane cardiac analysis. Code is available at: https://github.com/moeinheidari7829/P2Echo.

Abstract (英文): Feed-forward 3D reconstruction (3R) from unconstrained multi-view images has recently emerged as a prominent research direction in 3D computer vision. Various foundational reconstruction models have been proposed with different architectures and reconstruction paradigms. Despite being trained on large-scale perspective datasets with substantial computational resources, these models generally struggle to generalize to non-perspective images. This limitation hinders their deployment in applications involving arbitrary, unknown, and potentially heterogeneous camera models. To build on the capabilities of existing reconstruction models with minimal retraining, we propose Camera3R, a low-rank adaptation framework that efficiently extends perspective-pretrained 3R networks to any other camera models. Extensive experiments demonstrate that, with only 0.5% additional parameters and less than a day of training, Camera3R adapts a foundational 3D reconstruction model to non-perspective cameras while fully preserving its original performance on perspective inputs. Abstract (英文): This paper tackles the task of hand-object contact transition detection in egocentric video, which aims to localize the two temporal boundaries of a contact episode: the moment a hand first establishes contact with an object (touch) and the moment contact is released (release). We propose a unified framework that jointly models the interacting hand and object to accurately localize both transition types. The proposed framework augments hand-centric spatiotemporal representations with object-aware features learned directly from the video and introduces a transition-aware event spotting formulation tailored to contact transitions, without relying on external object or hand-object interaction detectors during inference. To support this task, we present ContactTransition, a dataset comprising 4,021 egocentric videos with 8,498 touch and 8,077 release annotations, together with object-of-interest supervision. Experiments demonstrate that the proposed method consistently outperforms strong two-stage and end-to-end event-spotting baselines on both transition types. Finally, we show that detected contact transitions provide effective temporal anchors for downstream egocentric hand-object reasoning.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Event cameras record asynchronous changes in brightness. To use a general visual model as a recording trigger, a system must represent these measurements in a form the model can interpret. We compare direct grayscale accumulation, two temporal encodings, and two intensity reconstructors on paired real event data. On 192 windows from four held-out DSEC recordings, thresholds calibrated only on development data yielded 40 of 52 person detections and 12 of 140 background positives with E2VID. The strongest direct temporal encoding at an admissible fixed cutoff yielded 14 detections and seven background positives. A retrospective comparison at a common 10% background-positive cap yielded 40 detections for E2VID and at most 19 for any direct encoding. FireNet+ had lower preparation cost and produced 21 detections with zero observed background positives at its frozen cutoff. On the same 192 windows, Qwen3-VL-4B yielded 33/52, 2/140 with E2VID and 9/52, 9/140 with the highest-recall direct rendering, the three-bin image (person detections, background positives). A 96-second exploratory replay illustrates the sensitivity of episode capture and recording starts to cutoff selection. The experiments identify a detection benefit from reconstruction under controlled input history and distinguish it from the cost and false starts of continuous triggering.

Abstract (英文): Open-Vocabulary Scene Graph Generation (OV-SGG) requires models to recognize visual relationships beyond the training vocabulary, yet existing methods often rely on dataset-specific object--predicate co-occurrence patterns. Under long-tailed distributions and noisy object localization, such reliance leads to biased, entangled relation representations, limiting both rare-relation recognition and generalization to unseen predicates. To address these challenges, we propose RaRe, a reasoning-aware relational representation learning framework that transforms MLLM-generated chain-of-thought (CoT) rationales into structured relation embeddings. Rather than using CoT only as an intermediate reasoning trace for final prediction, RaRe directly optimizes the rationale-derived representation space to improve inter-relation discriminability. Specifically, RaRe first aligns rationale embeddings with textualized relation descriptions through self-supervised contrastive learning, and then refines rationale generation with a specificity-oriented reinforcement learning objective that encourages semantically object-pair-specific relational evidence. Experiments on VG150 and PSG demonstrate improved category-balanced recognition over existing baselines, while the additional gains from learning vary across datasets and proposal settings. Code available at: https://artanic30.github.io/project_pages/RaRe.

</details>

Abstract (英文): SDR-to-HDR reconstruction is an ill-posed problem in which clipped highlights, crushed shadows, and quantization artifacts must be recovered while preserving the original content. Latent diffusion models provide powerful generative priors for restoring missing detail, but their effectiveness is constrained by how HDR signals pass through the pretrained VAE. We find that common log-like encodings suppress low-luminance chromatic differences before generation, producing a dark-side collapse in the frozen-VAE latent. We introduce EquivFlow, a compact, invertible exposure-equivariant encoding that maps exposure changes to additive shifts while retaining a scale-independent chromatic component, without modifying the VAE. Encoding-level tests across pretrained image and video VAEs show that its exposure structure and deep-dark chroma transport persist across the tested hosts. Integrated into SDR-to-HDR video diffusion, EquivFlow delivers consistent chromatic-fidelity gains across the tested settings while retaining competitive reconstruction fidelity.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Remote sensing imagery acquired by Unmanned Aerial Vehicles (UAVs) supports wide range of applications; however, deploying machine learning (ML) models on resource-constrained aerial platforms introduces challenges beyond predictive accuracy, particularly when deployment data differ from the distributions observed during training. Such shifts are common in remote sensing due to geographic, sensor, seasonal, and other visual variations. A practical solution is to equip deployed models with mechanisms that detect emerging distribution shifts and support subsequent responsive actions. This paper presents an empirical case study of three open-source detection tools: (i) Alibi-Detect; (ii) NannyML; and (iii) PyTorch-OOD. Using cross-dataset remote-sensing shifts, we evaluate both detection effectiveness and computational latency of these tools. Our results reveal differences in detection behavior and processing cost across methods and implementations, highlighting trade-offs between detection capability and response-time. These findings motivate further work toward lightweight, and latency-aware monitoring mechanisms for resource-constrained systems.

Abstract (英文): Curvilinear structures such as retinal vessels and surface cracks need both pixel-level accuracy and structural continuity. Pixel-wise supervision does not model the connections between pixels, and topology-based losses still produce ordinary masks. Connectivity-based methods predict these connections directly, but they use binary connectivity targets over a fixed local range. Such labels give the same value to thin segments and to thick regions, and they cannot describe connections at larger offsets. To address these limitations, we propose Topology-preserving Multi-offset Affinity Learning (TMAL). TMAL uses soft affinities that emphasize thin regions near the boundary, and it predicts directional affinities at several offsets to reach further. It then combines these affinities with weights that come from the decoder features, so local continuity and wider context are used together. An auxiliary segmentation branch adds region-level supervision and keeps the overlap accuracy high. On the retinal vessel dataset DRIVE and the crack inspection dataset DeepCrack, TMAL reaches the highest Dice, IoU, and clDice scores, and it greatly reduces the connected-component error $Δβ_0$. Code is available at https://github.com/suho-han/TMAL.

</details>

Abstract (英文): We propose a novel conditional entropy function as a basis for quantifying predictive uncertainty in Evidential Deep Learning (EDL) for glaucoma detection from retinal fundus images. Using the EyePACS-AIROGS-light-V2 and RIM-ONE-DL datasets for validation, we found that our proposed EDL model regularized by conditional entropy function produced uncertainty estimates with substantially lower calibration errors than Deep Ensembles, MC Dropout, post-hoc calibration and the conventional EDL framework based on Dempster uncertainty. To further probe our models' interpretability, we analyzed Grad-CAM heatmaps using a focus index representing spatial concentration. Compared with the other uncertainty quantification methods, EDL models exhibited greater separation between the index distributions of correctly and incorrectly classified fundus images. In particular, false negatives were associated with localized Grad-CAM activations that were displaced from the optic disc, consistent with its established clinical importance in glaucoma assessment. Overall, our findings suggest that our proposed EDL framework for glaucoma detection provides not only enhanced uncertainty calibration but also admits a more physiologically consistent interpretability.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Diabetic retinopathy (DR) lesions vary widely in shape, and microaneurysms (MAs) span only a few pixels. We hypothesize that modeling the spatial organization of lesion regions, with global context and shape-adaptive scanning, improves the segmentation of such small and irregular lesions. To test this, we propose D$^2$Mamba, which couples non-causal state space duality with deformable Mamba scanning. We evaluate it with five-fold cross-validation on the DDR dataset, comparing against representative segmentation baselines under an identical training protocol, and conduct ablations on each component. D$^2$Mamba achieved the best overall performance, with 33.87% mIoU, 2.99 percentage points above the strongest baseline. The largest gain appeared on MAs, the smallest and most easily missed lesions, where Dice improved by 10.06 percentage points. These results confirm our hypothesis that spatially adaptive state space modeling benefits tiny-lesion segmentation. Code and pretrained weights are available at https://github.com/PUREPANEL1/D2Mamba.

Abstract (英文): Unified multimodal models (UMMs) have opened up new possibilities for generating rich interleaved text-image content. However, they generally lack native support for coordinating the two modalities, and translating coarse user requests into coherent multimodal compositions also requires explicit planning. We propose UniWeaver, a structured, model-agnostic harness framework that integrates multimodal planning, generation, and visual feedback to produce coherent interleaved outputs. To train UMMs for this task, we construct UniWeaver-10K, a dataset of 10,000 tasks across 20 task families, paired with execution trajectories guided by UniWeaver. We also introduce UniWeaver-Bench, a task-oriented benchmark systematically assessing text coverage, writing quality, visual coverage, and image-text alignment. Experiments show that UniWeaver achieves state-of-the-art performance and improves interleaved text-image generation across multiple UMM backbones.

Abstract (英文): Traffic cameras continuously record urban road-user behavior, providing proactive safety evidence beyond crash reports. Yet safety-critical events - conflicts, near misses, unsafe turns, sudden braking, and crosswalk interactions - are rare and brief, buried in hours of routine footage. Existing traffic-video pipelines extract detections, trajectories, and risk scores but process footage uniformly, without adapting to an analyst's query. Vision-language methods improve scene understanding but often lack traffic-specific verification of participant-level conflicts. We introduce AgenticConflictLens, an agentic, query-conditioned framework for efficient, auditable conflict extraction from long fixed-camera traffic videos. Given a natural-language safety query, a Traffic-Aware Controller combines structured traffic memory with specialized conflict-analysis tools to choose where to search, which road users to examine, and which evidence to verify or reject. A coarse-to-fine search retrieves candidate intervals and applies detailed verification selectively. The framework produces conflict records with refined time intervals, participant pairs, semantic regions, trajectories, motion evidence, uncertainty, rejection reasons, and supporting clips. On 96 annotated hours from Bellevue and NYC DOT, AgenticConflictLens achieves the highest conflict-existence F1 and temporal IoU on both datasets: 0.84 F1/0.77 tIoU on Bellevue and 0.80 F1/0.73 tIoU on NYC DOT, while inspecting only 8.7% and 11.9% of video, respectively. These results support scalable, evidence-grounded urban traffic-safety analysis.

</details>

Abstract (英文): As text-to-video (T2V) models are increasingly positioned as world simulators, it is essential to evaluate whether their videos conform to physical commonsense. However, existing benchmarks rely on annotators who rate each video with a single score based on intuition, yielding ratings that are subjective and do not tell which physical principle is violated. We introduce PhyRubric, a rubric-grounded diagnostic framework for evaluating physical commonsense in T2V generation. For each caption, PhyRubric constructs a caption-specific rubric before any video is seen, decomposing the caption into semantic and physical items, and derives scores from item-level verdicts grounded in timestamped evidence. We instantiate this framework as PhyRubric-Bench, which spans 34 categories across five physical domains, and scale it up to PhyRubric-Train with 3,788 videos, on which we fine-tune the open-source PhyRubric-Judge. Empirical results across ten T2V models show that physical commonsense remains far from solved, and that PhyRubric-Judge correlates more closely with human judgments than existing open-source judges while matching the frontier proprietary Gemini 4 Argon. Further analysis provides actionable insights for T2V models.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The morphology of astrocytes is closely associated with their ability to support and modulate neural circuit functions; however, its reconstruction from volume electron microscopy (vEM) data remains labor-intensive and computationally resource-intensive, placing substantial barriers on its broader adoption and scalability despite the high spatial resolution of vEM. We present AstroSeg-vEM, a lightweight, astrocyte-centric framework for morphology-preserving astrocyte segmentation, 3D reconstruction, and quantitative morphological analysis in vEM images. AstroSeg-vEM combines transformer-based 2D segmentation with dedicated post-processing methods and operates directly on high-resolution EM sections, making it well suited to anisotropic vEM data and downstream proofreading workflows while preserving the full in-plane resolution of the original EM data. Across four mouse hippocampal datasets representing normal and morphologically defined stress-associated astrocyte ultrastructural states, AstroSeg-vEM demonstrates robust performance and generalization, achieving average Dice and weighted Dice values of 0.92 and 0.95, respectively, on a held-out test dataset. Its lightweight architecture enables efficient, high-fidelity reconstruction on consumer-grade hardware, providing a scalable and accessible platform for large-scale astrocyte ultrastructure analysis and future astrocyte-neuron interaction studies.

Abstract (英文): Generative video compression offers a promising solution for compressing and reconstructing videos under extremely low bitrate constraints, among which the diffusion-based solutions still achieve the best performance by providing the powerful generative latent prior. However, their effectiveness critically depends on when the compressed latent is introduced into the diffusion process, which directly governs the trade-off between reconstruction fidelity and perceptual realism. To address this limitation, we identify denoising start-timestep selection as a key factor governing this trade-off and formulate it as a geometric optimization problem. Based on this formulation, we propose a novel adaptive diffusion entry method for generative video compression (ADE-GVC), the first timestep-aware generative video compression framework, featuring a lightweight predictor that estimates an adaptive denoising start point from the compressed latent. A temporal adapter further extracts compression-aware conditions and injects them into a pretrained video diffusion model, enabling the reconstruction of videos with both high fidelity and realism. More importantly, we observe a strong quantitative relationship between the predicted timesteps and the distortion between the original and compressed latents, providing empirical support for our optimization formulation. Extensive experiments demonstrate that ADE-GVC achieves the state-of-the-art performance on perceptual metrics and substantially improves reconstruction realism over existing generative video compression methods. Notably, our ADE-GVC achieves an average bitrate savings of 40.99\% (on DISTS) and 95.24\% (on PSNR) compared to the latest open-source baseline GLC-Video.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Image manipulation localization must jointly exploit semantic structure and noise-sensitive forensic evidence. However, the effects of spatial correspondence, matching quality, and interaction order are often conflated. Existing semantic-forensic fusion methods provide complementary representations, but do not explicitly preserve quality-mismatch information during spatial correspondence or adaptively select the order in which information is propagated across branches. We propose PUMA (Probability-constrained Unbalanced Matching and Aggregation), which combines spatially constrained unbalanced optimal transport with position-wise convex selection between two sequential operators through a shared positional interface. Before normalization, PUMA retains signed transport-mass residuals, constructs row-stochastic cross-branch and within-noise-branch operators, and updates noise features through a gated residual path. PUMA achieves strong localization results on four test sets, with scores on COVERAGE and NIST16 exceeding the published results of the representative methods listed in our comparison. The experiments support the utility of local matching states and adaptive sequential interaction for cross-source localization and several post-processing conditions.

</details>

Abstract (英文): 3D human motion generation plays a crucial role in game development, extended reality (XR), digital humans, and embodied simulation. Beyond motion generation, practical pipelines include rigging, retargeting, and rendering, making ultra-fast long-term 3D motion generation without compromising quality valuable for real-time interactive applications. Despite promising progress, high-quality long-horizon motion generation under tight inference budgets remains challenging, owing to 2 coupled bottlenecks: (1) generative strategies that require many sampling steps for high-quality 3D motion generation and (2) model architectures with substantial per-step computational and memory costs for long sequences. We present FlashMotion, a co-designed framework for high-quality long-horizon 3D motion generation under a strict inference budget, covering text-to-motion, music-to-dance, and speech-to-gesture tasks. To enhance trajectory-level efficiency, we introduce a 3D motion-space MeanFlow formulation with kinematic and dynamic consistency constraints for few-step generation, together with an Adaptive Soft masking mechanism for temporally coherent motion editing. To improve per-step efficiency, we design a lightweight non-autoregressive BiMamba architecture with condition-guided state selection and AdaIN-based condition fusion mechanism, enhancing cross-modal alignment and generalization to longer motion sequences. Extensive experiments demonstrate that FlashMotion achieves overall state-of-the-art performance in both quality and efficiency across text-to-motion, speech-to-gesture, and music-to-dance generation. Code is available at FlashMotion (https://github.com/Sun-Happy-YKX/FlashMotion).

</details>

Abstract (英文): Recently, 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) has emerged as an efficient representation for novel view synthesis, offering fast differentiable rendering and high visual fidelity, and has increasingly been extended to surface reconstruction. However, the relationship between depth information and reconstruction error remains insufficiently explored, limiting the principled design of geometric optimization strategies. To fill the above gap, we propose DepthGS, a depth-guided Gaussian splatting framework. We derive theoretical relations between depth discrepancies and reconstruction error under single-view and multi-view settings, and rigorously establish corresponding upper bounds on reconstruction error. The above analysis informs the design of adaptive regularization strategies in both single-view and multi-view optimization. More specifically, for single-view optimization, we propose a depth-difference-guided adaptive normal regularization strategy that modulates the normal constraint strength using a geometric reliability weight computed from local depth differences. For multi-view optimization, we introduce a cross-view depth consistency regularization strategy that promotes consistency between neighboring-view depth maps and the corresponding depth predictions obtained by transforming the current view's depth predictions into those neighboring views. Extensive experiments on DTU, Mip-NeRF360, and Tanks and Temples demonstrate that DepthGS achieves competitive performance in both surface reconstruction and novel view synthesis.

</details>
