Abstract (英文): Retrieval-augmented systems often retrieve passages that are accurate and relevant but state the answer at a coarser level of granularity than the question requires. For example, a question may ask which specific disease a drug treats (familial hyperlipidemia), while the retrieved passage mentions the broader disease family (hyperlipidemia). Prior work on context sufficiency treats such coarse passages simply as insufficient. We argue that they form a distinct case, which we call resolvable insufficiency: text alone cannot settle the fine-grained answer, but the broader category it mentions maps to a short list of candidate answers in a domain ontology. When language models read these coarse, family-only passages, they exhibit a phenomenon we call Clade (Category-Level Answering from Deficient Evidence): they answer instead of abstaining or searching further. On biomedical questions from PrimeKG, family-only passages reduce abstention by 13.8 points compared to uninformative passages, and the resulting guesses are wrong about half the time. A counterfactual test confirms the cause: replacing an exact disease name with its broader family name lowers abstention by 17.4 points compared to deleting the sentence. Simple fixes like confidence filtering, scaling to a larger model, and explicitly instructing the model to abstain cannot resolve Clade, and this replicates on ecological species interactions in GloBI. Finally, we test whether an ontology can turn coarse passages into exact answers. Given the correct family, an ontology list includes the true answer in 98.5% of cases, but string matching finds that family on the page for only 19.8%. Letting the model choose the family raises this to 30.0% and end-to-end recovery from 10.0% to 26.8%. Overall, our results show when language models should stop guessing and consult an ontology, and highlight which steps of that process remain unsolved.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Large language models (LLMs) increasingly exhibit capabilities that resemble human Learning by Thinking (LbT), the acquisition of new insight through internal processes such as explanation and reasoning, without new external input. A systematic account of the cognitive mechanisms behind these capabilities, and of their limits, remains underdeveloped. This paper proposes a Cognitive LbT Framework that maps four mechanisms (Reflective Reasoning, Synthetic Memory Formation, Internal Simulation, and Conceptual Self-Organization) onto computational processes in LLMs. Drawing on representative methods, including chain-of-thought prompting, self-consistency, self-reflection, Tree-of-Thoughts search, Self-Instruct, and self-taught reasoning, the analysis argues that selected methods can functionally approximate aspects of these mechanisms and can improve performance on some tasks without additional labeled examples, although this does not always constitute durable learning in model parameters. For example, Self-Instruct generated approximately 52,000 instructions from 175 seed tasks and reported a 33% absolute improvement on Super-NaturalInstructions (Y. Wang et al., 2023). The same mechanisms also produce characteristic failures, including hallucination diffusion, model collapse, concept drift, and high computational cost. These failures are not isolated technical defects but follow from four structural asymmetries between LLMs and human cognition: the identity of generator and monitor, closed learning loops without external grounding, symbolic rather than sensorimotor simulation, and the cost of inference-time computation. The framework yields testable propositions about when LbT in LLMs should succeed or fail and offers a structured basis for designing more reliable autonomous learning systems.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Energy-based models (EBMs) define a distribution through a scalar energy, $p_θ(x)\propto\exp(-E_θ(x))$, and are usually presented as one model family among many. This tutorial has a broader goal: beyond introducing EBMs, it uses them as a single lens that connects the different aspects of machine learning. Any EBM is specified by three choices, the energy, which determines what the model represents; the inference procedure, which samples from or optimizes the energy; and the learning method, which fits the energy to data. We first develop these components, including sampling, variational inference, and learning by maximum likelihood, noise contrastive estimation, pseudolikelihood, score matching, and adversarial training, and show that the learning methods share one contrastive structure. We then show that discriminative models, generative models such as GANs, normalizing flows, diffusion and autoregressive models, representation learning methods such as contrastive learning, word2vec, BERT, denoising auto-encoders, and diffusion representations, reinforcement learning with EBM policies and world models, inverse reinforcement learning, and planning, as well as inference-time computation with verifiers, are all EBMs that differ in these three choices, mostly in how they handle the partition function and sampling. This view explains existing methods, organizes them into a common framework, and suggests new algorithms by replacing one component while keeping the others, with composition of energies as a principled way to combine learned models at inference time.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Local language models can save memory by storing weights in fewer bits, and they can reduce sensitivity to the order of answer options by averaging a few reorderings. We ask whether the first makes the second worse, and which is the cheaper way to spend a fixed budget. On Qwen3-0.6B and Qwen3-1.7B with simulated round-to-nearest weight quantization (8, 6, 4 and 3 bits, fp16 reference), read from the logits of the letters A to D on ARC-Easy, ARC-Challenge, MMLU and HellaSwag (300 items each), 6 bits lose no accuracy against fp16 (they score +1.8 (+1.0 to +2.5) and +0.9 (+0.3 to +1.5) points higher, which we do not explain), and 8 bits change it by -0.7 (-1.1 to -0.3) and -0.4 (-0.7 to -0.2) points. At 4 bits the answer-slot skew rises from 0.15 to 0.53 (0.6B) and from 0.07 to 0.12 (1.7B); Gaussian noise at the same weight SNR gives 0.15 and 0.37 (two draws) and 0.19 (one draw), so the control is inconclusive on whether rounding adds slot skew beyond equal-power noise. Flip rates rise but stay below an accuracy-matched baseline of independent uniform errors. Averaging four cyclic orders recovers +12.6 points at 4 bits for 0.6B (+5.3 at fp16). We price bits and passes in one machine-independent unit, weight bytes read times passes. In cost-matched pairs, extra orders beat extra bits only when the weights have 6 or more bits, and the decision table reduces to: use 6-bit weights and spend the rest of the budget on up to four orders, one pass each. Everything is simulated on two small models with the simplest quantizer; a title-level search found no earlier study of quantization together with option order.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Local decoding usually fixes one weight precision for the whole generation. We study a different allocation: store 8-bit weights, decode with their top 4 bits, and redo a step at 8 bits only when the low-precision output distribution looks uncertain (entropy above a threshold or a small top-two margin). Quantization is simulated, the model is Qwen3-0.6B, and the benchmark is GSM8K. Cost is counted in weight bytes read per token. On 36724 replayed steps the entropy of the low pass ranks argmax flips with AUC 0.93. At a 15% escalation budget it catches 78% of flips and the margin gate 94%, against the 15% expected for a random gate of equal rate (a computed expectation, not a run). In 150 live problems all-high scores 64.0% and all-low 55.3% (McNemar p=0.053). An entropy gate with a 16% escalation rate scores 62.7% at 0.87 of the all-high weight bytes. Its paired difference is -1.3 (-8.0 to +5.3) points to all-high and +7.3 (+0.0 to +14.7) points to all-low (p=0.080), so the live data do not separate the gate from either end. Abstract (英文): Formal world models can support planning, verification, and safety reasoning, but constructing them from open-ended natural-language situations is difficult, particularly in multi-agent settings where several formalizations may be plausible. We study whether five LLMs can generate such world models as partially observable stochastic games and whether mechanical validity is sufficient to establish that the resulting model faithfully represents the intended decision problem. We introduce a benchmark of 44 scenarios spanning game, situational, and cultural settings and evaluate generated world models along three dimensions: mechanical validity, reference-relative structural agreement, and semantic criteria. Across three empirical tracks, we analyze 410 first-pass generations. In the reference-backed game track, 134/135 generated world models pass a strict mechanical audit, yet mean permutation-invariant structural F1 is only 0.366, with just 28/134 mechanically valid models exactly matching the authored reference. In the cultural track, explicitly naming the country changes the generated structural representation for most LLMs while producing only small changes in automated semantic scores. These results show that executability is a necessary but insufficient criterion for LLM-generated world models: models that are mechanically valid can still differ substantially in the structure used by downstream neuro-symbolic reasoning and planning.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Research ideation is iterative: researchers refine their ideas step by step based on feedback. Yet existing evaluations obscure this search process, making it difficult to understand how LLMs explore the space of research ideas and what limits their progress. We introduce Re-Search, which adapts the format of Twenty Questions to research ideation: given only the research problem of a published paper, a model must rediscover the paper's core idea by asking yes-or-no questions. Built from 200 ICML 2026 papers, Re-Search tracks how a model's idea evolves as it asks questions and receives binary oracle feedback, isolating ideation from execution. Across six recent LLMs, nearly all progress happens within the first 30 of 100 questions, which largely decide how the game ends; after that, most models plateau. Through controlled interventions on the candidate questions and the hypothesis space, we find that the bottleneck is generation, not judgment: models recognize a good idea or a good question when one is offered, but often cannot produce it on their own. We further analyze the ideation traces and find that models fail by either over-exploiting one direction or over-exploring too many. These findings point to concrete design principles for autoresearch systems: since models can judge candidates they cannot produce, enriching the search with externally sourced ideas and questions may be more effective than improving any single model's generation ability.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Business process automation runs the same Standard Operating Procedure (SOP) again and again on new inputs. Large Language Model (LLM) agents can follow an SOP written in natural language, but they call an LLM to choose every step of every run, which adds latency and cost at scale. We present ReTrace, which turns an SOP and a few logged agent runs into a reusable workflow in which code, not an LLM, selects the next step. The agent's reasoning, the explanation it writes with each tool call, often states why it took an action; the tool calls alone do not. ReTrace extracts the condition behind each step, merges these conditions across runs into a compact rule document, and generates an executable workflow from it. Only this last stage depends on the platform, so rule documents can be compiled for different orchestration platforms. On $79$ procedures from our production platform, ReTrace workflows reach $93.8\%$ completion, against $96.8\%$ for the agent, at $24\%$ lower cost per task. On $τ^2$-Bench, they reach $75.8\%$ task success (agent: $80.6\%$) and run in $1.3$s instead of $170$s; three rounds of repair on training failures lift $k{=}2$ workflows from $66.7\%$ to $81.2\%$. On eight SOP-Bench procedures, ReTrace without repair trails few-shot generation ($80.6\%$ vs. $83.6\%$), but repair from labeled training failures lifts it to $87.8\%$ at $0.40$s per task, compared to $85.5\%$ in $46.6$s for the agent.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Deploying LLM-based analytics agents in enterprise settings requires evaluation frameworks that can reliably detect failures across complex, multi-tool workflows. We present a three-phase comparative study of three evaluation frameworks, each representing a distinct evaluation paradigm (trace-based LLM judging, text-only LLM judging with red-teaming, and deterministic heuristics), applied to two analytics agents in a controlled research setting using frozen execution traces. Phase 1 quantifies evaluation harness stability through repeated measurements, finding that 97% of configurations meet the stability threshold and that stability patterns are agent-independent. Phase 2 measures known risk detection on 72 ground-truth test cases, where the trace-based framework achieves the best precision-recall balance (F1=0.90) while text-only evaluation matches on recall but generates 5x more false alarms. Phase 3 evaluates unknown risk discovery, uncovering 24 novel failures with zero overlap between functional testing and security red-teaming. Our results demonstrate that (1) the information available to the evaluation harness determines evaluation quality, (2) functional and security evaluation are complementary, and (3) LLM-based evaluation harnesses surface subtle errors that human reviewers miss. We recommend a two-framework strategy: trace-based evaluation for functional assessment and adversarial red-teaming for security validation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): LLM agents are increasingly used for open-ended, data-driven scientific discovery, yet most of them evaluate hypotheses pointwise, testing an empirical claim $h$ only against its complement $\neg h$. When evidence rejects $h$, the agent learns that one explanation is unlikely but not which alternative the evidence supports. To recover that alternative, we introduce Contrastive Discovery, which replaces the single hypothesis with a contrast set, a scientific question paired with mutually exclusive and collectively exhaustive competing explanations. Each experiment is scored by how its evidence redistributes belief across the set, and the question is resolved when belief concentrates on one explanation. Because contrast sets change only the unit an agent proposes and scores, an existing agent can use them to re-evaluate evidence it has already collected or to guide its search. Across six domains, $89\%$ of the pointwise candidates with the largest belief shifts are rejections. On the same evidence, contrast sets resolve $73\%$ of these candidates to a specific alternative. When contrast sets guide search, the agent resolves $67\%$ more questions than pointwise surprisal under the same experiment budget. In a blinded study, $38$ human experts chose the same explanation on $78\%$ of resolved questions and on $82\%$ of questions where the pointwise agent rejected its hypothesis and stopped. Together, these results support explicit competing explanations as a way for discovery agents to resolve more scientific questions from the same evidence and validation budget.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Satellite-based wildfire spread studies differ in data organization and evaluation procedures, making model performance difficult to compare. We constructed an event-level benchmark to compare model performance and examine how the models used the available inputs. The benchmark covers California during 2012 to 2021 and combines VIIRS 375 m active-fire observations, MODIS NDVI-derived vegetation information, and ERA5-Land wind fields. Each wildfire event was represented on a fixed 100 × 100 grid. Each sequence sample used ten historical daily steps to predict the next-day satellite-observed fire-state mask. Six spatiotemporal models (CNN-LSTM, Vision Transformer, ConvLSTM, Pairwise-ConvLSTM, Patchwise-ConvLSTM, and ST-GNN) were evaluated using common input definitions, preprocessing procedures, prediction targets, and metrics. Integrated Gradients was applied after training to quantify attributions from the five raster input channels to the next-day model outputs. Pairwise-ConvLSTM achieved the lowest overall RMSE of 0.0495 on the cumulative wildfire dataset. Among the five evaluated Patchwise-ConvLSTM configurations, the 7 × 7 setting achieved the highest observed F1-score (0.955), with precision (0.962) and recall (0.949). On the newly burned-pixel subset, the same configuration achieved precision (0.916), recall (0.888), and F1-score (0.902). The attribution analysis showed larger mean absolute values for vegetation-constrained unburned states (7.23 × 10−5) and prior cumulative fire history (5.13 × 10−5) than for newly detected fire pixels (1.81 × 10−5), the eastward wind component (7.01 × 10−6), and the northward wind component (8.97 × 10−6). The benchmark provides a common framework for assessing predictive performance and model input use in next-day satellite-observed wildfire spread prediction.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Drought increasingly constrains ecosystem functioning in semi-arid regions, yet the nonlinear thresholds through which vegetation restoration regulates carbon–water processes and ecosystem resilience remain poorly understood. In this study, a drought–carbon–water–resilience framework was developed to assess how vegetation restoration affects ecosystem drought resilience in the Mu Us Sandy Land, a representative semi-arid sandy ecosystem in northern China. MODIS products, including MOD17A2H/MOD17A3H for gross and net primary productivity (GPP and NPP), MOD16A2 for evapotranspiration (ET), MOD13A2 for the normalized difference vegetation index (NDVI), MOD11A2 for land surface temperature (LST), and MOD44B for vegetation cover, were used for the period 2001–2020 and harmonized to a 1000-m spatial resolution and an annual temporal scale to quantify water use efficiency (WUE), carbon use efficiency (CUE), the temperature–vegetation drought index (TVDI), ecosystem resistance, recovery, resilience, and a restoration intensity index (RII). The results showed that drought intensity increased slightly over time, while WUE and CUE exhibited contrasting spatial patterns and different sensitivities to drought. At the pixel scale, temporal correlations between annual TVDI and WUE/CUE were positive over 83.74% and 69.78% of the study area, respectively, indicating that carbon–water use efficiency often showed compensatory increases under mild-to-moderate drought conditions. In contrast, the regional drought-gradient analysis revealed threshold-dependent responses of WUE and CUE to increasing TVDI, with critical thresholds at approximately TVDI = 0.67 and TVDI = 0.66, respectively; beyond these thresholds, both water and carbon use efficiency declined rapidly. Strong restoration areas showed higher RSwue and RScue than weak/no restoration and degradation areas, and the proportion of high-resilience areas increased with restoration intensity, particularly for CUE-based resilience. Hierarchical regression further showed that RII provided additional explanatory power for ecosystem resilience after controlling for drought intensity, although its effects differed between water-use and carbon-use processes. These findings indicate that vegetation restoration enhances drought resilience through threshold-dependent carbon–water regulation, but its effectiveness depends on vegetation structure and water availability. Threshold-based monitoring and structure-optimized restoration should therefore be prioritized in semi-arid sandy ecosystem management.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Vegetation dynamics in drylands fundamentally restructure the land-atmosphere interface, yet the resulting surface water-energy coupling across saline-alkali environments remains insufficiently understood due to the complex interplay between climatic aridity and edaphic salinity. By integrating multi-decadal satellite observations with a biogeophysical decomposition framework, we quantified the growing-season impacts of vegetation enhancement (cropland expansion and grassland restoration) alongside desertification across the saline-alkali drylands of northern China from 2003 to 2020. Our results demonstrated that vegetation enhancement induced significant localized surface cooling, peaking in barren-to-cropland conversions (−0.674 ± 0.02 K), followed by grassland-to-cropland conversions (−0.066 ± 0.007 K) and barren-to-grassland restoration (−0.017 ± 0.009 K), whereas desertification exhibited a subtle warming tendency (+0.005 ± 0.0003 K). These thermal responses were primarily governed by non-radiative, evapotranspiration-mediated processes. Furthermore, the salinity-aridity nexus modulated regional energy partitioning through two divergent hydro-edaphic pathways. In reclaimed irrigated cropland, management interventions alleviated ambient matric and osmotic constraints, while intensifying regional water consumption. In contrast, non-irrigated restored grasslands exhibited a functional decoupling between canopy greening and evaporative cooling, reflecting conservative stomatal regulation enforced by compound soil matric and osmotic stresses. The marginal warming accompanying desertification indicated an impaired surface evaporative buffer following vegetation loss and secondary salinization. By elucidating how the salinity-aridity nexus modulates land-surface water-energy coupling, these findings highlight the necessity to align restoration strategies with local hydro-edaphic capacities, balancing the microclimatic cooling of irrigated agriculture against the long-term hydrological sustainability of natural grasslands under accelerating global change.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Long-term satellite monitoring of rift propagation is essential for assessing Antarctic ice shelf stability and projecting sea-level rise. Currently, long-term tracking of rifts still relies on time-consuming manual interpretation, significantly affecting its feasibility for large-scale rift monitoring. Here, we present a novel framework for automatically tracking rift propagation, which fuses the fine-tuned visual foundation model (Segment Anything Model, SAM) and spatiotemporal correction. The SAM enables automated rift extraction using only a few samples without any manual prompts, while the spatiotemporal correction improves the precision of rift mapping for geometric measurements. We applied this framework to automatically monitor key rifts on the Amery Ice Shelf using 768 Landsat 8/9 and Sentinel-2 images from 2015 to 2025, achieving an F1 score of 98.6% across 11 independent scenes. Rift length and width measurements were in agreement with manual interpretation, with R2 values of 0.979 and 0.999, respectively. Furthermore, our extended monitoring revealed recent structural changes within the rift system on the Amery Ice Shelf. This study offers a new way for automated rift tracking with the foundation model for the first time, having significant potential for long-term monitoring of pan-Antarctic rifts.

Abstract (英文): Modelling spatial interactions remains a core issue in geographic information science. Faced with the emerging, non-stationary spread of epidemics, the effects and uncertainties of spatial interactions are amplified. Limited by reliance on a single spatial interaction prior, existing models perform well in specific scenarios but suffer from prediction drift across multi-phase epidemics, thereby hindering real-time and dynamic policy adjustments. To address this limitation, we propose an ensemble approach driven by spatial interaction, built upon a foundational metapopulation model. Using the 2022 Shanghai Omicron outbreak as a case study, we evaluated the proposed ensemble model against 14 mechanistic and machine learning baselines. In multi-phase prediction tasks, the proposed ensemble model achieves the best overall performance with mean ranks of 3.25 for RMSE and 4.00 for R2, surpassing all baselines with mean ranks ranging from 5 to 15. Furthermore, it improves mean predictive accuracy by 29% to 72% compared with the non-ensemble models, while reducing the prediction error interval by 10% to 84%. Finally, we generated model-based projections across multiple counterfactual scenarios to explore responses to changes in intervention timing and intensity.

Abstract (英文): Accurate river discharge estimation is essential for water cycle modeling, water hazards prevention, water ecosystem protection and water resource management. However, conventional ground-based gauging networks are often sparsely distributed and insufficient to capture the complex hydrodynamic characteristics within large and heterogeneous river basins. With the rapid advancement of remote sensing technologies, particularly the launch of the Surface Water and Ocean Topography (SWOT) mission, new opportunities have emerged for large-scale and continuous river discharge estimation. In this study, we develop and evaluate a SWOT-based river discharge estimation framework for the Yangtze River by integrating SWOT RiverSP hydraulic observations organized on the SWORD river network with Satellite Embedding V1 data in a random forest model to capture both hydrodynamic and geomorphological characteristics. The framework is evaluated using temporally separated training and testing observations from nine gauging stations along the Yangtze River mainstream, with station-specific nonlinear empirical models used for comparison. The main conclusions are as follows: 1) The random forest model substantially improves discharge estimation relative to the empirical formulation, with a maximum NSE gain of 0.4372 and an NRMSE reduction of up to 0.1175; 2) The model demonstrates reliable performance across the nine gauging stations, with all stations achieving NSE ≥ 0.72 and the overall performance reaching NSE = 0.8910 and NRMSE = 0.0664; 3) The spatially explicit discharge estimates show strong agreement with reach-scale results (correlation ≈ 0.9), indicating that water level exerts primary control on discharge, while channel width and geometry significantly modulate discharge responses in morphologically complex reaches. These results demonstrate the potential of combining SWOT hydraulics with satellite foundation embeddings to improve discharge estimation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Single-point annotation in oriented object detection of remote sensing scenarios is gaining increasing attention due to its cost-effectiveness.However, due to the granularity ambiguity of points, there is a significant performance gap between previous methods and those with fully supervision. In this study, we introduce P2RBox, which employs point prompt to generate rotated box (RBox) annotation for oriented object detection. P2RBox employs the SAM model to generate high-quality mask proposals. These proposals are then refined using the semantic and spatial information from annotation points. The best masks are converted into oriented boxes based on the feature directions suggested by the model. P2RBox incorporates two advanced guidance cues: Boundary Sensitive Mask guidance, which leverages semantic information, and Centrality guidance, which utilizes spatial information to reduce granularity ambiguity. This combination enhances detection capabilities significantly.To demonstrate the effectiveness of this method, enhancements based on the baseline were observed by integrating three different detectors. Furthermore, compared to the state-of-the-art point-annotated generative method PointOBB, P2RBox outperforms by about 29% mAP (62.43% vs 33.31%) on DOTA-v1.0 dataset,which provides possibilities for the practical application of point annotations.

Abstract (英文): Perennial crops are a key component of sustainable agriculture due to their higher carbon storage compared to annual systems. Improving their mapping is essential to support effective public policies. This study proposes an integrated approach combining field observations, waveform LiDAR data from Global Ecosystem Dynamics Investigation (GEDI), and hyperspectral imagery from EnMAP to enhance perennial crop classification.Field data were used to develop an interpretation key for the analyzed classes. GEDI Level 1B waveforms were normalized to extract metrics such as the number of peaks, while structural attributes, including canopy height (RH95), were derived from GEDI Level 2A. From EnMAP imagery, spectral reflectance was obtained and processed using mean values, first- and second-order derivatives, and continuum removal. Differences among classes were evaluated using Tukey’s test and principal component analysis (PCA). Results show that waveform structure and canopy height significantly distinguish secondary vegetation from perennial crops. Additionally, spectral regions in the visible and shortwave infrared present significant differences between these classes. These findings demonstrate that integrating structural and spectral variables improves the discrimination of perennial crops and helps overcome challenges in separating them from secondary vegetation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Global Navigation Satellite System (GNSS) radio occultation (RO) has evolved into a heterogeneous multi-mission observing system that combines national, partner, and commercial constellations. This review and outlook synthesize NOAA/STAR assessments of GNSS RO commercial data, including Spire and PlanetiQ, and compares their quality with those of national missions, COSMIC-2, MetOp, and other missions. We emphasize the results in the neutral atmosphere. To examine heterogeneous multiple RO missions, we organized the comparisons along a pathway from i) orbit and viewing geometry through antenna gain and received signal, ii) examining their signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) and penetration, iii) quantifying the phase quality, retrieval bias and uncertainty, iv) demonstrating their impacts on numerical weather prediction (NWP), and climate and environmental applications. The reviewed comparisons show that higher SNR generally improves tracking and lower-tropospheric penetration but does not by itself remove biases and uncertainty arising from water-vapor irregularity, multipath, super-refraction, departures from spherical symmetry, or processing choices. Spire and PlanetiQ retrievals are broadly compatible with COSMIC-2 and partner missions in the principal atmospheric layers, and their additional profiles improve spatiotemporal sampling and provide incremental value for global and tropical-cyclone prediction. For long-term environmental records, however, operational usefulness is not sufficient: common processing, inter-mission stability assessment, sampling correction, and mission-specific uncertainty characterization are required. Multi-mission RO observations support upper-troposphere and lower-stratosphere temperature records, tropospheric water-vapor variability and trend analyses, and planetary-boundary-layer height estimates when these conditions are met. The synthesis yields an application-conditioned evaluation framework that separates signal quality, retrieval quality, usable observation yield, sampling complementarity, and downstream impact. The principal outlook priorities are uncertainty-aware quality control and error specification, improved treatment of super-refraction and lower-tropospheric retrievals, sustained multi-mission reprocessing and intercomparison, and observing-system design that balances measurement quality with coverage.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Remote sensing image captioning enables automated interpretation of remote sensing data for applications such as environmental monitoring and urban planning. However, general-purpose multimodal models exhibit suboptimal adaptability on remote sensing imagery due to domain discrepancies in data distribution, resolution, and rotation invariance. We propose a domain-specific memory bank-enhanced framework for remote sensing image caption, built upon the lightweight EVCap architecture with three core innovations: (1) a remote sensing specific visual memory bank providing domain-representative visual-semantic alignment; (2) a staged transfer training strategy that leverages pre-trained knowledge while mitigating overfitting from limited remote sensing data; and (3) a K-means clustering-based few-shot memory bank that preserves performance while reducing memory footprint for resource-constrained deployment. Extensive experiments on NWPU, RSICD, and Sydney datasets demonstrate substantial improvements over baselines. Specifically, CIDEr increases by 3.3% on RSICD (0.2735 to 0.2825) and 11.7% on Sydney (0.1422 to 0.1589). The proposed method also achieves competitive performance against state-of-the-art methods while maintaining favorable computational efficiency.

Abstract (英文): Abstract Red crown rot (RCR), caused by Calonectria ilicicola , is an emerging soybean disease in the U.S. Midwest for which scalable approaches to characterize within-field disease distribution are lacking. This study evaluated high-resolution PlanetScope satellite imagery for mapping RCR-affected soybean canopies across 15 commercial fields in Illinois surveyed during the 2024 and 2025 growing seasons. A total of 2,921 georeferenced canopy plots were classified as asymptomatic or RCR-affected and paired with six multispectral bands and seven vegetation indices. Spectral differences between classes were evaluated using linear mixed-effects models, and seven machine-learning classifiers representing linear, tree-based, neural-network, kernel, and probabilistic approaches were compared using spatially independent leave-one-field-out cross-validation. RCR-affected canopies exhibited increased reflectance in the visible and red-edge regions, reduced near-infrared reflectance, and lower vegetation-index values relative to asymptomatic canopies. All classifiers showed strong discrimination, with ROC-AUC values ranging from 0.963 to 0.982. Regularized logistic regression achieved the highest overall performance, with an accuracy of 0.945, balanced accuracy of 0.945, F1-score of 0.948, and ROC-AUC of 0.982 at the optimized decision threshold. Permutation analysis identified EVI, NDVI, and red reflectance as the most influential predictors across representative model architectures. Satellite-derived probability and classification maps generally corresponded with symptomatic canopy patterns observed in high-resolution UAV imagery, although mixed pixels reduced precision near disease-patch boundaries. These results demonstrate the potential of high-resolution satellite imagery for within-field mapping of RCR-associated canopy symptoms across independent commercial soybean fields.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Coastal water levels during storms result from interacting tides, storm surges, waves, currents, bathymetry, and coastal geometry, producing spatially heterogeneous responses that remain difficult to characterize from point observations alone. The Surface Water and Ocean Topography (SWOT) mission provides a new opportunity to investigate this complexity through wide-swath High-Resolution (HR) observations of sea surface height (SSH). Here, SWOT HR water levels are first evaluated against five tide gauges in the English Channel and subsequently analyzed at three contrasting sites, Cherbourg, Villers-sur-Mer, and Étretat, using complementary spatial and scale-resolved approaches. SWOT HR shows strong agreement with tide gauges at the best-performing sites, with RMSE values of approximately 0.11 m and correlations exceeding 0.99, while accuracy remains site-dependent. Beyond point-based validation, the two-dimensional HR fields reveal pronounced spatial heterogeneity, including localized and elongated SSH anomalies with distinct geometries and orientations. EOF decomposition shows that more than half of the variance remains beyond the first six modes at all three sites, highlighting the complexity of the HR spatial signal. The detrended SSH anomalies show organized spatial patterns, while their point-by-point linear correlations with radar backscatter (σ0) remain close to zero. Across the three investigated coastal domains and two storm events, the two-dimensional wavelet analysis identifies enhanced SSH variability within an energetic scale range of approximately 0.5–1.0 km, although its intensity, spatial distribution, and organization differ substantially among sites. Comparison with independent wave, surface-current, and bathymetric fields further places these observations within contrasting hydrodynamic and morphological environments, suggesting that the observed sub-kilometer SSH variability may be associated with local coastal processes, although their individual contributions remain unresolved. . Together, these analyses demonstrate the added value of SWOT HR for resolving the two-dimensional, multiscale complexity of storm-time coastal SSH, providing new spatial information for investigating coastal dynamics and supporting high-resolution model validation and data assimilation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Ground surface subsidence resulting from underground mining is characterized by significant spatial deformation gradients and nonlinear temporal deformation. A single monitoring technique is insufficient to simultaneously achieve extensive spatial coverage and high temporal continuity. Interferometric Synthetic Aperture Radar (InSAR) provides high spatial resolution but is limited by sparse temporal sampling, whereas the Global Navigation Satellite System (GNSS) offers dense temporal sampling but with restricted spatial coverage. To address these limitations, this study introduces a spatio-temporal fusion method combining InSAR and GNSS, based on baseline deformation field–residual decoupling (BR-STKF), specifically applied to the Banji Coal Mine in the Huainan Mining Area, Anhui Province. Sentinel-1 SBAS-InSAR is employed to derive Line-Of-Sight (LOS) deformation time series, while three-dimensional GNSS displacements are projected onto the LOS direction. A daily scale baseline deformation field is constructed using pixel-wise Kalman filtering and Rauch–Tung–Striebel (RTS) smoothing. Subsequently, residual signals between the InSAR/GNSS observations and the baseline field are estimated utilizing a correlation-anchored multiscale spatial basis and an information-form Kalman filter. Helmert variance component estimation is introduced to adaptively adjust the weights of InSAR and GNSS observations. To demonstrate the reliability of the proposed framework, a quantitative validation was conducted. The experimental results yielded root mean square error (RMSE) values of 6.37 mm for strict spatial leave-one-out cross-validation (LOOCV) and 2.84 mm for retrospective reconstruction with missing InSAR phases. These results are competitive and comparable to leading benchmarks such as the Spatio-Temporal Kalman Filter (STKF), while achieving lower mean RMSEs than baseline methods such as Spatio-Temporal Random Effects (STREs), Enhanced Spatio-Temporal Random Effects (eSTREs), Improved Spatio-Temporal Random Effects (ISTREs), Multi-resolution Orthogonal Polynomial Basis (MOP), and ST-Kriging. However, the statistical stability of differences among models requires further verification with additional independent data. The findings suggest that the baseline deformation field–residual decoupling strategy effectively integrates the spatial coverage of InSAR with daily GNSS observations, offering a viable framework for daily scale line-of-sight (LOS) deformation reconstruction in mining areas.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Compound temperature and soil moisture extremes threaten terrestrial carbon uptake, but their contrasting ecological impacts remain poorly understood. We assessed dry–hot (DH), wet–hot (WH), dry–cold (DC), and wet–cold (WC) events across China from 2001 to 2025. The analyses combined daily air temperature, root-zone soil moisture, and MODIS-derived gross primary productivity (GPP). We quantified event occurrence, negative-response probability, cumulative GPP loss, and recovery time, and examined environmental predictors using explainable machine learning. WC events were most frequent, whereas DH events lasted longest. WH frequency increased over the study period, while DC frequency declined. DH and DC produced negative GPP responses in 63.5% and 67.1% of classifiable events, respectively. Among negative-response events, DH had the largest national mean cumulative event-period GPP loss below the −0.5 SD threshold (2.85 g C m−2) and the longest conditional recovery time (3.17 days). WC losses exceeded DC losses despite a lower negative-response probability. Losses varied with elevation, season, and event severity. Vapor pressure deficit dominated predictions of DH negative responses and losses, whereas other event types showed greater contributions from radiation, precipitation, and soil texture. These findings highlight the need to distinguish event occurrence, response probability, productivity loss, and recovery when assessing ecological risks from compound extremes.

</details>

LiDAR enables rapid high-precision point cloud acquisition, yet single-platform data have limitations: airborne laser scanning (ALS) cannot capture sub-canopy structure, while terrestrial laser scanning (TLS) cannot fully acquire canopy information. Based on 92 plots in Guangxi, this study proposes a weighted point-to-normal iterative closest point (WPN-ICP) registration algorithm for high-precision fusion of ALS and TLS point clouds, with comparisons to trimmed iterative closest point (TrICP) and iterative closest point (ICP). An adaptive density-based spatial clustering of applications with noise (DBSCAN) algorithm was developed for diameter at breast height (DBH) estimation, and a DBH cluster-based vertical layering method (DCVL-THE) for tree height estimation. Three segmentation-based methods—canopy height model (CHM), region growing (RG), and comparative shortest path (CSP)—were selected for comparison. WPN-ICP achieved a mean MAE of 0.0359 m, with reductions of 85.87%, 69.03%, and 73.00% compared with coarse registration, TrICP, and ICP. Using the fused point cloud, DBH estimation achieved an RMSE of 0.6326 cm with an RRMSE of 5.18%, and tree height estimation achieved an RMSE of 0.7160 m with an RRMSE of 6.58%. Compared with the suboptimal CSP method, the RMSE and RRMSE for DBH were reduced by 41.79% and 41.68%, and for tree height by 25.66% and 25.68%. This method does not require full individual-tree or crown segmentation, thereby avoiding the error propagation associated with such segmentation and enabling accurate stand-scale estimation.

Abstract (英文): Hyperspectral image (HSI) classification is a fundamental remote sensing task, but the high-dimensional nature of HSI data poses challenges for acquisition, storage, transmission, and processing. To mitigate these issues, researchers have developed compressive classification methods based on the coded aperture snapshot spectral imaging (CASSI) system, enabling direct classification in the compressed domain without reconstructing the full hyperspectral cube. However, existing methods often fail to fully leverage spatial-spectral correlations, suffer from limited multi-scale feature extraction, and are constrained by fixed receptive fields and degradation caused by compression. To overcome these limitations, we propose ECSA-UNet, an end-to-end framework that directly classifies compressive spectral measurements from CASSI. The model introduces a spatial-spectral decoupling module and refines the U-Net architecture with global–local feature extraction, Spatial-Spectral Attention, a Multi-Scale Enhancement Pyramid and Cross-Stage Connections, enabling dynamic receptive fields, improved long-range dependency modeling, and robust local–global feature fusion. Moreover, the CASSI coded aperture is parameterized as a trainable component, allowing for joint optimization of coding and classification within an end-to-end learning paradigm. Extensive experiments on four benchmarks show ECSA-UNet achieves state-of-the-art accuracy, and ablations confirm each module’s contribution. In addition, it maintains an effective trade-off between parameter overhead and computational efficiency, offering a viable solution for latency-sensitive hyperspectral remote sensing tasks.

</details>

Abstract (英文): To address scale-like errors in local point clouds caused by pitch angle errors in unmanned aerial vehicle-borne LiDAR bathymetry (UAV-ALB) systems, this study proposes a correction method based on scanline keypoint constraints. First, the point cloud is traversed using scanline information to extract geometric keypoint features from each scanline. Next, a scale-like error correction model is constructed based on the distance differences between keypoints at the start and end of overlapping scanlines, and the pitch angle correction value is estimated using a forward–backward fusion approach. The estimated correction is then applied to the attitude data, and the point cloud is reprocessed. This procedure is iterated until the convergence criterion is satisfied. Experimental results show that, after correction, the horizontal root mean square errors (RMSE) of the two datasets decrease from 0.217 and 0.283 m to 0.025 and 0.030 m, respectively, while the elevation RMSE values decrease from 0.227 and 0.241 m to 0.006 and 0.004 m, respectively. These results demonstrate that the proposed method effectively mitigates scale-like errors in UAV-ALB point clouds and substantially improves their geometric consistency and elevation accuracy.

Abstract (英文): Forest canopy height is a key parameter for estimating forest biomass and carbon stocks, as well as a fundamental indicator for assessing forest ecosystem health. Although existing 10 m resolution global canopy height products are free and open-source, they suffer from substantial systematic errors due to the inherent biases of spaceborne LiDAR and the nonlinear relationship between optical imagery and canopy height, making them insufficiently accurate as direct training labels for deep learning. This study proposes a complete framework of “adaptive stratified screening, followed by weakly supervised UNet training and minimal ALS post-processing refinement.” First, using the 12 spectral bands of Sentinel-2 multispectral imagery, the OOB distance is calculated via weighted random forest as an anomaly metric. Through systematic comparison of three anomaly metrics (Cook’s distance, OOB distance, and standardized residuals) and five retention ratios (30–50%), the OOB distance at 40% retention is identified as optimal. An adaptive retention strategy based on tree height stratification is then implemented: for low-tree areas (<20 m), 40% of low-anomaly samples are uniformly retained; for tall-tree areas (≥20 m), the retention ratio is dynamically adjusted according to the proportion of tall-tree pixels (95% when tall-tree proportion < 10%, 70% when 10–30%, and 50% when ≥30%). The screened high-confidence Lang data are then used as supervisory signals to train a UNet model for predicting canopy height across the entire region, producing an initial canopy height map (MAE = 6.97 m, Bias = +4.21 m). Finally, using only a minimal amount of airborne LiDAR data (down to 0.1% area proportion), with a 14-dimensional input comprising the initial predicted height, screening mask values, and the 12 spectral bands of Sentinel-2, a multilayer perceptron is independently trained for each region to perform post-processing refinement of the initial predictions. Experiments based on 68 independent regions from the Open-Canopy dataset, with a strict regional split (34 validation/34 test), show that post-processing refinement reduces the test set MAE from 6.97 m (ASS) to 3.29 m (0.1% ALS, a 52.8% improvement, 58.1% improvement over the original Lang product), Bias from +4.21 m to −0.13 m, and RMSE from 8.95 m to 4.72 m. Even when the ALS proportion is reduced to 0.01%, the MAE remains 3.57 m (a 48.8% improvement). Statistical significance testing (paired Wilcoxon signed-rank test, n = 34) indicates that ASS+MLP(0.1%) significantly outperforms ASS (p < 0.00001). This study provides an effective technical pathway for regional refinement of global canopy height products.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The HY-2B/C/D constellation delivers routine altimeter significant wave height (SWH) and sea-surface wind speed over the Chinese coastal seas through four product streams (GDR, IDR, ODR, and SDR), yet whether the three satellites are mutually consistent, and whether that consistency is the same for both parameters, has not been established. We assess three years (2023–2025) of 1 Hz Ku-band retrievals against 30 quality-controlled coastal buoys, using a deduplicated matchup database, a common-buoy and common-period protocol with bootstrap confidence intervals, and triple collocation against ERA5. Within each satellite, the product streams are consistent for both parameters: the reprocessed GDR shifts the operational SWH by a sea-state-independent −0.157 m and the wind streams coincide to rounding. Across satellites, consistency is parameter-dependent. For SWH, the biases on common buoys span 0.18 m; HY-2B has the lowest random error, significantly lower than HY-2D, and HY-2C cannot be separated from either in random error. For wind speed, HY-2C reads about 1 m s−1 low in all four Chinese seas and in every wind band once the bands are defined by an independent reference, so that most of the offset is removable by a constant. The near-shore degradation of SWH inside 50 km largely reflects the sub-0.5 m sea states that dominate the near-shore buoys, and wind speed shows no comparable coastal signal. We translate these findings into applicability bounds and a parameter-aware recipe for merging the constellation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV) thermal infrared remote sensing can rapidly acquire thermal-response information for large-scale photovoltaic (PV) arrays. Nevertheless, local thermal anomalies occupy small regions within aerial images; their features tend to degrade during successive down-sampling. Moreover, model deployment on edge devices is constrained by limited computational and memory resources. To address these challenges, this paper proposes TP-YOLO, a photovoltaic anomaly-detection approach, and implements it on the Zynq-7020 hardware platform. First, a three-channel thermal-prior input consisting of luminance distribution, local luminance difference, and a luminance gradient is constructed from pseudo-color infrared images, and explicitly characterizes thermal anomalies without requiring absolute-temperature measurements. Next, the detection scales of YOLOv11n are adjusted to P2/P3/P4. Modules including A-C3k2, Conv + Add, and TLCB are adopted to optimize feature extraction and multi-scale fusion, preserving local anomaly features while restraining model complexity. On the hardware side, a PS/PL co-processing architecture is employed. A dedicated convolution IP, ADD IP, and Window IP are designed for convolution, element-wise fusion, and local-window operations, respectively. INT8 quantization and ping-pong buffering are further utilized to realize network deployment. On the PV HSD test set, TP-YOLO achieves an mAP@0.5 of 77.5% and an mAP@0.5:0.95 of 33.7%, with 1.91 M parameters and 6.2 GFLOPs computational cost. In cross-dataset evaluation on the Drone Dataset without fine-tuning, the mAP@0.5 reaches 64.4%. After deploying the INT8 quantized model onto Zynq-7020, the implemented system yields an mAP@0.5 of 76.8%, achieving single-frame inference latency of 162 ms and throughput of 6.17 FPS at 150 MHz, with a power consumption of 3.4 W.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Safe and reliable carbon capture and storage (CCS) requires long-term monitoring to verify secure CO2 containment. Distributed acoustic sensing vertical seismic profiling (DAS-VSP) provides a practical approach for subsurface monitoring due to its permanent installation, dense spatial sampling, and reduced operational costs. However, monitoring programs spanning decades may require hardware upgrades, including interrogator replacements and changes to fibre-optic cable configurations, affecting time-lapse repeatability and interpretability. This field case study evaluates whether the CO2-induced 4D DAS-VSP response remains interpretable across a combined change in interrogator, fibre type and optical path, whose individual contributions cannot be isolated. The assessment uses data from the CO2CRC Otway Project Stage 4, where approximately 10 kilotons of CO2-rich reservoir gas were injected into a saline aquifer at approximately 1500 m depth. Baseline and monitor surveys using different DAS configurations are compared at two monitoring wells, with a same-configuration case as a repeatability benchmark. An established 4D DAS-VSP processing workflow, supplemented by system-specific noise attenuation, is applied to mitigate configuration-dependent differences. The results show that, despite higher non-repeatability, the principal CO2-related time-lapse anomaly remains spatially localised and distinguishable, indicating that the DAS-VSP response can remain interpretable across a combined configuration change under favourable acquisition and signal-quality conditions.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Long-term monitoring of wetland degradation, recovery, and spatial resilience requires methods that can jointly exploit multi-temporal remote sensing observations and spatially heterogeneous environmental information. This study develops an intelligent remote sensing framework for monitoring the disturbance–recovery dynamics and spatial resilience of typical wetland landscapes in Northeast China by coupling Landsat-based Detection of Trends in Disturbance and Recovery (LandTrendr) with geographically weighted random forest (GWRF). Annual wetland landscape state sequences for 2000–2022 are constructed from multi-temporal Landsat observations and associated geospatial variables. LandTrendr time-series segmentation is used to identify disturbance timing, disturbance magnitude, recovery rate, and recovery duration, thereby converting long-term wetland change trajectories into quantitative indicators of degradation and recovery. At the GIS grid scale, remotely sensed vegetation and wetness indicators are integrated with wetland patch structure, hydrological conditions, topography, land-cover change, and anthropogenic pressure. GWRF then models local nonlinear relationships under spatial-distance weighting to generate a spatially explicit wetland resilience index and reveal geographically varying dominant controls. The LandTrendr–GWRF model achieved an R2 of 0.872, an RMSE of 0.057, and an MAE of 0.039, outperforming RF, GWR, XGBoost, and standalone GWRF benchmarks. The mean wetland resilience index was 0.641, with low-, medium-, and high-resilience areas accounting for 27.86%, 44.37%, and 27.77% of the study area, respectively. Significant spatial clustering was detected (Moran’s I = 0.421, Z = 6.92, p = 0.001). Hydrological regulation, landscape connectivity, fragmentation constraint, and anthropogenic disturbance pressure emerged as the four dominant spatial response types. High-resilience cells formed contiguous clusters where hydrological recharge and landscape connectivity were strong, while low-resilience cells clustered where fragmentation and construction pressure were concentrated. Disturbance magnitude and recovery duration differed markedly among the four response types, with connectivity-dominated cells returning to the pre-disturbance state within 4.4 years and anthropogenic pressure-dominated cells requiring 11.7 years. The proposed framework extends wetland remote sensing from static condition mapping toward process-oriented monitoring of degradation, recovery, connectivity, and resilience, providing a practical approach for long-term wetland restoration assessment and spatial management.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Aboveground biomass (AGB) is considered a key variable in surveilling the sustainability of forest management and the carbon cycle. This study examined the effects of image acquisition season and topographic variables on the performance of AGB estimates using Sentinel-2 imagery from three seasons. The study was conducted in 224 sample plots in pure Calabrian pine (Pinus brutia) stands in southern Türkiye. Random forest models were trained on 26 datasets derived from rainy, arid, and semi-arid season imagery, including single-season, two-season, and three-season combinations with and without topographic variables. Models trained using satellite images from rainy and semi-arid seasons performed better than models trained using data from the more arid season. All two-season combinations outperformed the corresponding single-season models, but none reached the accuracy of the three-season model. Including topographic variables reduced RMSE for every season and season combination. The best results were obtained using all three seasons together with topographic variables (R2 = 0.74, RMSE = 13.97 Mg ha−1), and removing topography from this model increased RMSE to 15.69 Mg ha−1. According to the SHAP analysis, elevation was the second-most influential predictor after Aug_SAVI (mean SHAP value = 1.65 Mg ha−1) and the only topographic variable that notably contributed to predictive performance. These findings indicate that, for the pure Calabrian pine stands and conditions examined in this study, combining multi-seasonal Sentinel-2 imagery with topographic variables improves AGB estimation accuracy compared with single-season models and spectral data alone.

</details>

Spectral channels are first analyzed through machine learning-based feature importance analysis, and normalized spectral features are subsequently constructed. These features are then combined with auxiliary variables, including meteorological conditions, observation geometry, and surface environmental factors, to develop an XCH4 retrieval model. The GOSAT L2 XCH4 product is introduced as a high-accuracy reference observation for correcting the retrieved XCH4 results, and independent validation is conducted using observations from the TCCON site. Among the evaluated models, XGBoost demonstrates the best performance, achieving an R2 of 0.91 and an RMSE of 9.28 ppb under five-fold cross-validation. The model also maintains relatively better predictive performance compared with other models under both temporal and spatial extrapolation scenarios. Compared with the TROPOMI L2 product, the retrieved XCH4 shows improved agreement with TCCON observations, with a higher correlation (R = 0.84) and reduced errors. Based on this approach, a daily, spatially continuous XCH4 dataset over China for 2021 is generated. Analysis of the retrieved dataset reveals pronounced spatiotemporal patterns, characterized by higher XCH4 concentrations in eastern China and lower values in the western regions, as well as a distinct seasonal cycle.

Abstract (英文): Landslides are among the most serious geohazards worldwide, and accurately identifying their locations is a prerequisite for effective risk prevention and mitigation. However, regional-scale screening of candidate active-slope units from InSAR observations is still hindered by subjective manual interpretation, inconsistent deformation-rate thresholds, and insufficient topographic constraints. To address these limitations, this study proposes a largely automated terrain-constrained framework for candidate active-slope screening by integrating InSAR observations, slope units, and screening based on slope-unit bootstrap intervals and a regional two-standard-deviation background envelope. First, surface deformation rates are derived from InSAR measurements and post-processed to reduce the influence of identifiable artifacts before slope-unit analysis. Then, a clustering-based method is introduced to delineate topographically constrained slope units in alpine canyon regions, whose visual terrain correspondence is examined qualitatively using terrain information and optical imagery. Next, rather than applying a fixed site-specific deformation-rate cutoff in mm/yr, data-dependent bootstrap confidence-interval comparisons are used to determine whether the deformation interval of each slope unit extends beyond the regional deformation-background interval, thereby enabling rule-based screening of candidate active-slope units. This confidence-interval screening rule provides a quantitative criterion for regional screening but requires further calibration across additional geomorphic and deformation settings. Finally, the proposed method is applied to two landslide-prone regions in western China, located in sections of the Upper Yellow River and Upper Jinsha River, where final inventories containing 92 and 25 candidate active-slope units are produced, respectively. These polygons are geomorphologically constrained candidate active-slope units produced for regional screening and should not be interpreted as the exact boundaries of individual landslides. Inventory-based spatial comparison and targeted optical inspection were reported as complementary evidence streams rather than as independent validation of contemporaneous slope activity. These results document implementation in two related alpine river-valley settings in western China and indicate the framework’s potential as a quantitatively defined, topographically constrained approach for largely automated regional screening of candidate active-slope units in comparable settings. These two applications do not establish broad applicability across substantially different environments.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Recent blind hyperspectral image unmixing methods have achieved effective spectral–spatial representation, yet the reliability of estimated abundances remains difficult to assess because errors in jointly estimated endmembers and abundances can compensate for each other. To address this issue, we propose Residual Diagnosis-Guided Abundance Correction (RDAC), a post-correction framework that preserves the initial abundance estimate as an anchor and characterizes its mismatch using structured reconstruction residuals. Abundance candidates are then combined with structured residual information, while only 1% reference abundances are used to learn the correction. RDAC was evaluated on the Jasper Ridge, Samson, and Urban datasets. With UNMamba as the primary blind-unmixing backbone, RDAC achieved A-RMSEs of 0.0229, 0.0175, and 0.0359, reducing the initial errors by 55.05%, 70.80%, and 59.63%, respectively. It also achieved the lowest mean A-RMSE among the compared methods on Jasper Ridge and Samson while remaining competitive on Urban. Further experiments showed that reconstruction error alone does not reliably indicate abundance accuracy. When applied to NMF, RDAC reduced A-RMSE by 80.45%, 89.25%, and 77.61% on the three datasets. The results indicate that structured residual diagnosis can support abundance correction under limited supervision, and the NMF experiment shows that the same correction framework can also be used with an alternative blind-unmixing backbone.

Abstract (英文): The characteristics and formation mechanisms of lightning activity during the lifetime of Super Typhoon Doksuri (2023) were investigated using ERA5 reanalysis data and observation data from the World-Wide Lightning Location Network (WWLLN) and Himawari-8/9 satellite. The lightning activity exhibited pronounced intermittent outbreak behavior, and intense lightning flashes mainly occurred in the outer rainband region approximately 200–600 km from the typhoon center. The occurrence of intense lightning was mainly related to the development of deep convections which were characterized by a high CAPE index and low cloud-top brightness temperature. In the landfall stage, intense lightning outbreaks may have been further modulated by the orographic lifting from the underlying surface and island thermal effects. Strong updrafts within the typhoon provided favorable conditions for intracloud microphysical electrification processes. The formation of intense lightning was interpreted via the non-inductive charging mechanism and validated using E-WRF model simulations. During the first rapid-intensification episode, intense inner-core lightning preceded the typhoon intensity peak by 14–41 h; during the re-intensification episode, it occurred ~9 h after the typhoon peak. These insights into the intense lightning activity in Super Typhoon Doksuri (2023) contribute to improving typhoon forecasting and disaster warning in the Western Pacific.

</details>

With total significant wave height included in every baseline, paired non-polarimetric and polarimetric configurations are evaluated on identical samples using a geophysical model function with minimum-variance fusion (GMF/MV), extreme gradient boosting (XGBoost), and a fully connected network trained by back-propagation (BP-FCN). Adding the degree of polarization and normalized third Stokes component reduces the overall root mean square error (RMSE) by 7.83%, 5.49%, and 5.31%, respectively, with the polarimetric BP-FCN configuration achieving the lowest RMSE of 1.666 m s−1. Wind speed-stratified analysis shows that the relative error reduction from polarimetric information varies non-monotonically across wind speed intervals. Across all signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) intervals represented in this study, all three methods achieve positive polarimetric gains, with paired month-block bootstrap 95% intervals lying above zero, indicating benefits across different SNR conditions. Under low-wind, high-swell conditions, RMSE decreases by 10.58%, 11.26%, and 9.82%, respectively, exceeding the overall relative gains. Error decomposition indicates that these improvements mainly reflect reduced systematic overestimation. Comparisons against ASCAT scatterometer and NDBC buoy winds using all collocated samples also show positive polarimetric gains of 4.83–5.35% and 4.80–5.78%, respectively. These results demonstrate that polarimetric observations can further improve GNSS-R wind speed retrieval accuracy beyond the information already included in the baselines, with more pronounced gains under the low-wind, high-swell conditions sampled in this study, providing useful complementary information for sea surface wind speed retrieval under complex sea states.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Drought affects meteorological forcing, root-zone water availability and land-surface functioning at different spatial and temporal supports. We evaluated Uruguay’s 2020–2023 drought using an observational 2016–2024 panel of 1423 SMAP parent cells. Monthly SPEI-3, SPEI-6 and SPEI-12 represented short, intermediate and persistent climatic water-balance deficits. SMAP root-zone soil moisture (RZSM) was retained at 9 km support, whereas actual evapotranspiration (ETa) and the moisture stress index (MSI) were summarized from approximately 500 m pixels using annual MapBiomas fractions for grassland, cropland and silviculture within each parent cell. For each cell and 8-day composite position, responses were expressed relative to the corresponding 2016–2019 mean. An interval was classified as locally dry when its overlap-day-weighted SPEI-12 was below −1. During 2020–2023, RZSM responses ranged from −13.09% to −16.63% among dominant landscape domains. Fraction-weighted ETa responses were −8.69% for grassland, −6.75% for cropland and −7.39% for silviculture, while oriented MSI responses were −6.62%, −3.32% and −11.20%, respectively. At lag zero, monthly RZSM correlated the most strongly with SPEI-3 (r = 0.81), followed by SPEI-6 (r = 0.64) and SPEI-12 (r = 0.51). At native 8-day support, RZSM was positively aligned with ETa (r = 0.42–0.57) and the oriented MSI (r = 0.31–0.59), although prewhitening weakened some associations. Overall, the 2020–2023 drought was consistently expressed across meteorological, root-zone and land-surface indicators. The strong association between SPEI-3 and RZSM, together with consistent land-surface departures and product-dependent ETa variation among components, highlights the value of multiscale monitoring for characterizing drought intensity and ecosystem responses across Uruguay.

Abstract (英文): Understanding and estimating the impact of atmospheric turbulence on the growth of precipitation particles in freezing weather can help improve the prediction of freezing rain precipitation. In this study, the turbulent kinetic energy dissipation rate (ε) during a freezing weather event was retrieved using the power spectral analysis method, based on observational data from a Ka-band millimeter-wave cloud radar, radiosondes, and ground automatic weather stations, and the influence of atmospheric turbulence on the growth of freezing rain precipitation particles was investigated using nonlinear causal methods such as transfer entropy (TE), convergent cross-mapping (CCM), the phase-randomized surrogate test, and partial CCM. Causality tests confirmed a significant bidirectional causal relationship between ε and Ze, with the information transfer from ε to Ze systematically dominating throughout the entire height range of precipitation particles. The bidirectional original ρ was far higher than the 99% quantile of the surrogate distribution, and the coupling was enhanced after removing the influence of temperature and humidity, indicating that it was independent of the thermodynamic covariant background. The causal intensity in the ice crystal growth zone above 6 km was approximately 3 times the average in the liquid zone, indicating that atmospheric turbulence is one of the key factors driving the growth of precipitation particles in freezing weather and that the effect is concentrated in the dominant layer of ice crystals. This finding provides a practical basis for improving the parameterization scheme and numerical prediction of the precipitation particle microphysical processes in freezing weather.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Hyperspectral imaging (HSI) is an important imaging modality in the field of optical remote sensing, capable of simultaneously capturing spatial structures and continuous spectral responses related to material properties. Therefore, compared with conventional red-green-blue (RGB) images, it can provide richer representations for understanding dynamic scenes. However, most existing video object segmentation methods have been developed for RGB videos, while high-quality hyperspectral video datasets with frame-by-frame pixel-level annotations remain scarce. To address this gap, this study constructed a hyperspectral video object segmentation dataset, comprising hyperspectral videos and their corresponding three-channel false-color RGB videos derived from the same HSI data, with pixel-level annotations provided for each frame. The dataset contains three spectral subsets: visible (VIS), near-infrared (NIR), and red-edge near-infrared (RedNIR). Using this dataset, we developed SSAM-SAM2, a hyperspectral video object segmentation network built on Segment Anything Model 2 (SAM 2). The network includes a spectral–spatial interaction enhancement (SSIE) module designed to identify informative spectral bands, suppress interference from redundant and noisy spectral channels, and integrate hyperspectral cues with the spatial-structure features of the corresponding false-color RGB images. In addition, the network introduces an Adaptive Correlation Memory Selection (ACMS) module that regulates memory sampling and selection according to variations in target state and inter-frame feature correlation. This design reduces the influence of redundant or unreliable historical information and improves temporal memory reliability under target deformation, motion variations, and background interference. Extensive experiments show that SSAM-SAM2 outperforms the selected comparison methods on the VIS, NIR, and RedNIR subsets under the evaluated settings. These results demonstrate the value of the proposed dataset and indicate that spectral–spatial enhancement and temporal adaptive memory mechanisms contribute to improving hyperspectral video object segmentation performance.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Small ship detection in synthetic aperture radar (SAR) imagery is limited by feature loss at coarse strides, sea-clutter false alarms, and the sensitivity of small boxes to annotation offsets. We propose CaSA-DETR, a detector built on RT-DETRv2 with three components. CFAR-Attention is a CFAR-inspired local contrast normalisation that reweights the stride-8 and stride-16 features bypassing the Transformer encoder, using sliding-window background statistics. Scatter-Aware Context Aggregation (SACA) collects context at four dilation rates with parallel dilated depthwise-separable convolutions and branch-adaptive gating. Inner-GIoU computes the regression loss on the inner 49% of each box, intended to reduce the influence of boundary offsets on targets smaller than 32 by 32 pixels. On a benchmark of 44,217 training images (73,407 instances, all under 32 by 32 pixels), the full model with a ResNet-101-vd backbone at 1024 by 1024 input reaches an average precision (AP) of 60.75% and an F1 score of 90.10%, 3.96 AP above D-FINE at the same input size. A cross of backbone, input size and modules shows that most of the gain over the original RT-DETRv2 baseline comes from the higher input, and that the three modules add 1.0 to 1.4 AP at every backbone and input size tested. On 2244 ship-free images, the modules cut false alarms by about 30% at a confidence of 0.5, at a cost of 0.66% more parameters and 2.3% more floating-point operations (FLOPs). The three modules improve AP at low cost.

</details>

Abstract (英文): In response to the application limitations of single-source navigation systems for their susceptibility to interference, insufficient positioning accuracy and limited scenario adaptability in complex environments, as well as the increasingly complex demands for high-precision, high-robustness, all-weather and all-scenario navigation services in fields such as the low-altitude economy, intelligent driving and specialized operations, multi-source fusion navigation technology is accelerating its evolution. This manuscript systematically reviews the development trajectory from reliance on a single navigation system to multi-source intelligent integrated navigation, dividing it into the following four evolutionary stages: single-system dominance, loose-tight coupling, deep integration and intelligent ubiquitous navigation stage. It focuses on a comparative analysis of the core characteristics, fusion modes, key technological breakthroughs, and practical limitations and bottlenecks of multi-source fusion technologies at each stage, with the aim of providing comprehensive reference for technological research and development, solution design and industrial implementation in related fields.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Satellite ocean-color remote sensing is used to detect harmful algal blooms (HABs), yet detection capability does not necessarily ensure reliable interpretation or transferable performance. If these limitations are overlooked when developing cross-regional or global automated HAB early-warning systems, biological claims may be overstated and regional changes in false-alarm or missed-detection rates may go unnoticed, potentially compromising warning reliability and related management decisions. We develop an evidence-constrained framework to evaluate both dimensions: reliability, defined by whether the observation–processing–inference chain supports the intended biological claim, and transferability, defined by whether task-relevant performance is retained under changes in time, region, sensor, or optical environment. A structured Web of Science search yielded 1212 unique records, from which 28 purposively prioritized full-text core studies underwent coding for validation design, cross-domain evidence, error, and uncertainty. The synthesis identifies an evidentiary gap between optical detection capability and biological claim specificity, while fixed-configuration cross-domain evidence was scarce and performance retention remained unquantified. Satellite observations can support bloom presence, optical phenotypes, and selected taxon-targeted applications, but specific claims require specific independent biological references. Reliability is constrained by reference and labeling practices, preprocessing, observation availability, and incomplete uncertainty characterization, while evidence for transferability is limited by insufficient source–target evaluation. We propose a framework linking claim–evidence alignment, independent validation, domain-shift assessment, and uncertainty reporting. Reliable and transferable HAB remote sensing should move beyond detection outputs toward evidence-supported, uncertainty-aware products for monitoring, early warning, and decision-making.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Building-footprint errors can propagate into geospatial assets generated from remote-sensing imagery. This study evaluated Mask2Former–FGMBRM, which applies feature-guided residual refinement to Mask2Former query-mask predictions and integrates extracted footprints with documented terrain data and geospatial processing for footprint-based urban asset generation. Six models were evaluated on 518 WHU held-out images using common final references and metric definitions while retaining model-specific pipelines. The tune-selected Mask2Former baseline and FGMBRM achieved AP values of 72.03% and 73.49%, respectively, a 1.47-percentage-point improvement. FGMBRM also achieved 89.01% binary IoU and 91.86% object F1, leading these measures and AP among the six configurations, whereas SegFormer had the highest boundary F1. Controlled ablations showed metric-dependent trade-offs, with simpler variants leading selected outcomes and no consistent advantage from query-conditioned guidance. The original INRIA comparison remained limited. In a separate query-capacity diagnostic, AP at common maxDets = 100 increased from 20.66% to 23.96%, while retaining more outputs increased recall and false positives. The results support footprint-based urban asset generation and visualization, with remaining dense-scene and geometric limitations.
