Abstract (英文): Adverse weather degrades visibility, contrast, edges, and local target details in aerial imagery, while small object size, viewing geometry, and onboard constraints further amplify detection failures. This review examines how weather-induced degradation propagates from image formation to feature damage and detection errors, and evaluates the evidence supporting existing robustness claims. A structured search of studies published between 2015 and 2026 identified 174 candidate records, which were analysed in terms of degradation mechanisms, methodological pathways, datasets, evaluation protocols, training dependencies, and deployment conditions. Existing methods are grouped into input restoration, joint restoration–detection learning, degradation-aware representation, domain adaptation and generalisation, multimodal and temporal compensation, and lightweight deployment. The evidence shows that improved visual quality does not consistently translate into better detection performance, while many reported gains depend on paired data, target-domain access, external priors, additional sensors, or computational overhead. Current evidence remains strongly concentrated on haze, with limited validation for natural rain, snow, dust, compound weather, unseen-weather generalisation, uncertainty, and closed-loop flight. Future research should therefore prioritise realistic weather data, controlled evaluation, risk-aware assessment, and onboard validation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Monitoring climate change is crucial to society, industry, and the environment to assess their vulnerability to such extreme temperature events and plan adaptation and mitigation strategies. Temperature-based climate indices, which are used for climate monitoring, are usually calculated using near-surface air temperature (NSAT) from weather stations. A limitation of NSAT is the inhomogeneity of station distribution across the globe, with some regions under-sampled. This study evaluates the potential of using land surface temperature (LST) retrieved via satellite to calculate temperature indices, because low Earth-orbiting satellites can observe almost all of the Earth. This work compares indices calculated from NSAT to those produced from LST, and the indices are aggregated by Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change AR6 regions. Results show that there is often moderate to high correlation between indices produced using NSAT and LST. In addition, it is found that monthly to seasonal changes in indices are similar for both variables, particularly in percentile-based indices. Threshold-based indices could not be calculated for some regions due to low data availability, and therefore this work also applies “relative” indices, where indices are normalised relative to the amount of data available. These generally also demonstrate high correlations between NSAT and LST and can better show when events, such as heatwaves, occur. Lastly, this work presents the potential of an LST-based temperature indices dataset and how uncertainties could be evaluated.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Inland lake ecosystems function as active nodes in the global carbon cycle, yet reliable basin-scale estimates of particulate organic carbon concentration (CPOC) remain scarce owing to the optical complexity of these environments. In this study, POC denotes the total particulate organic carbon pool, including organic matter from living and non-living particles. Here we present a semi-analytical retrieval scheme tailored to Sentinel-2 Multispectral Instrument (MSI) data and applied to 90 large eutrophic lakes (each >10 km2) within the Yangtze–Huaihe Plain (YHP) of eastern China. The scheme reconstructs the particulate absorption coefficient at 665 nm, ap(665), from reflectance measurements at 665, 705, and 740 nm, reducing the interference of colored dissolved organic matter (aCDOM) absorption and particulate backscattering (bb). When evaluated against 60 field observations in the predefined concentration-stratified holdout, the algorithm achieved MAE = 0.59 mg/L, RMSE = 0.73 mg/L, and MAPE = 46.89%—outperforming three widely cited benchmark algorithms—and showed preliminary transfer potential in the two estuarine datasets. MSI-derived CPOC maps from 2018 to 2024 revealed systematic east-to-west and center-to-periphery concentration gradients across the 90-lake assemblage, shaped by catchment nutrient delivery, basin hydrodynamics, and phytoplankton standing stock. Correlation and attribution analyses identified chlorophyll-a concentration (CChla), atmospheric pressure, and total phosphorus (CTP) as the three dominant environmental modulators of CPOC variability. The framework provides a field-calibrated approach for examining regional lake CPOC patterns. Its satellite application and ability to distinguish small concentration differences require further independent validation and uncertainty assessment.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Biomass burning smoke (BBS) emitted from Southeast Asia can be transported through regional atmospheric circulation, exerting significant impacts on air quality and climate over southwestern China. However, continuous observations of the vertical structures and optical properties of transported BBS remain limited. This study used one year of continuous multi-wavelength polarization Raman lidar observations at Mengzi (23.37°N, 103.38°E) in Yunnan, China, to identify transboundary BBS transport events and characterize their optical properties. Eight transboundary BBS events originating from Southeast Asia were identified using a comprehensive data processing framework involving aerosol extinction coefficient, backscatter coefficient, lidar ratio, particle linear depolarization ratio (PDR), and Ångström exponent. The transported smoke layers were mainly distributed below 4 km, exhibiting significantly enhanced aerosol extinction coefficients of 0.22–0.32 km−1 (compared with 0.02–0.06 km−1 for background aerosols). The 532 nm lidar ratios of the smoke layers (49.0–68.3 sr) were higher than those of the background aerosols (31.6–39.0 sr). The particle linear depolarization ratios ranged from 0.050 to 0.097 at 532 nm and from 0.14 to 0.19 at 355 nm, revealing a pronounced wavelength dependence. These values indicate measurable particle depolarization but do not independently establish particle morphology or the degree of smoke aging. Hybrid Single-Particle Lagrangian Integrated Trajectory (HYSPLIT) backward trajectory analysis together with fire hotspot observations revealed that this smoke mainly originated from biomass burning regions over the Indochina Peninsula and was transported to Yunnan across northern Myanmar, Laos, and northern Vietnam. This study provides important observational evidence of transport pathways, vertical distribution, and optical properties of transboundary BBS aerosols transported from Southeast Asia to southwest China.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Daily water-level changes in alpine closed lakes integrate lake-surface exchange, cryosphere processes, basin storage, and runoff routing, yet fixed calendar seasons and synchronous predictors may mix contrasting hydrological states and misalign antecedent forcing. This study developed a Hydro-Lag framework for Qinghai Lake, China’s largest inland lake, during 2014–2023 to improve daily reconstruction and driver attribution through dynamic hydrological process periods and time-lag effects. Satellite altimetry, optical and thermal imagery, hydrometeorological reanalysis, lake ice, and runoff data were integrated with water-balance constraints. The frozen (T1) and ablation (T2) periods were identified from lake ice transitions and a positive degree-day (PDD) threshold estimated from ice thickness. Meanwhile, basin wetness, represented by the antecedent precipitation index (API), distinguished the wet (T3) and dry (T4) periods. Candidate lags from cross-correlation analysis were refined through repeated cross-validation and used in an ensemble of extreme gradient boosting (XGBoost) and ExtraTrees; cross-fitted predictions were interpreted with SHapley Additive exPlanations (SHAP). Alignments differed markedly: selected core variables in T1 aligned mainly at 10–12 days; T2 used 1–2 days runoff, soil-moisture, and evapotranspiration offsets together with a same-day 14-day M_snow accumulation; T3 runoff and soil moisture at 3–5 days and precipitation-related variables at 8–9 days; and T4 antecedent basin variables at 11–24 days. Against an independently tuned SeasonRAW baseline, Hydro-Lag increased test R2 from 0.551 to 0.793 and reduced RMSE from 14.26 to 8.51 mm d−1. OOF SHAP indicated period-dependent patterns in modeled predictor contributions: mixed contributions in T1, wetness–storage emphasis in T2–T3, and antecedent storage and thermo-evaporative contributions in T4. Conditional responses showed thresholds and sign reversals across thermal, wetness, and event-magnitude gradients. The framework provides a process-aware basis for assessing daily lake–basin interactions in data-sparse alpine closed basins.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Fuel moisture codes are numerical indices used to estimate the moisture content of different layers of forest fuels and are widely applied to assess the ignition potential and behavior of wildfires. Terrain-corrected L-band ALOS-2 PALSAR-2 synthetic aperture radar (SAR) imagery predicted the Drought Code (DC) and Duff Moisture Code (DMC) of the Canadian Forest Fire Danger Rating System in mountainous forests of British Columbia. Random forest regression models were developed using backscatter, decomposition parameters, and polarimetric discriminators obtained from forest (high-biomass, conifer-dominated), sparse (medium-biomass, open canopy), and unforested (low-biomass, herbaceous-dominated) fuel types near weather stations. Out-of-bag predicted values were compared against weather station-derived values. Models trained using SAR data predicted the DC with moderate accuracy in sparse (R2 = 0.49, RMSE = 165.65) and forest sites (R2 = 0.39, RMSE = 173.80), and with low accuracy in unforested sites (R2 = 0.15, RMSE = 205.96). Models trained using SAR data predicted the DMC with moderate accuracy in sparse (R2 = 0.64, RMSE = 26.07) and unforested sites (R2 = 0.47, RMSE = 31.52), and with low accuracy in forest sites (R2 = 0.12, RMSE = 40.71). Incorporating SAR data and day of year (DOY) improved the accuracy of DC models. Errors in both DC and DMC increased when models were evaluated with SAR data from weather stations not used in training, suggesting limited spatial transferability beyond training weather stations. Results suggest that terrain-corrected L-band polarimetric SAR has potential for predicting fuel moisture in mountainous and forested environments. Differences in surface and soil conditions across the fire environment must be accounted for to improve the spatial transferability of the models.

Abstract (英文): Data assimilation generally assumes that observation-minus-background (OMB) departures follow an unbiased Gaussian distribution. Therefore, bias correction (BC) is essential for the effective assimilation of satellite radiances. This study applies four machine learning (ML) approaches to reduce systematic biases in clear-sky observations from the water-vapor-sensitive channels 9 and 10 of the Fengyun-4A (FY-4A) Advanced Geosynchronous Radiation Imager (AGRI). These methods include random forest (RF), extreme gradient boosting (XGBoost), multilayer perceptron (MLP), and the Kolmogorov–Arnold network (KAN). The machine-learning-based bias correction (MLBC) methods are evaluated for Typhoon In-Fa (2021) using the Weather Research and Forecasting Data Assimilation (WRFDA) system. Experiments without BC and with conventional variational bias correction (VarBC) are designed for comparison. Compared with VARBC, the four MLBC methods better capture nonlinear OMB biases and improve the statistical distributions of the departures. They produce more symmetric OMB distributions for channel 9 and distributions closer to the Gaussian distribution for channel 10, with smaller OMB STDVs and RMSEs than VARBC over both land and ocean for channel 10. After assimilation, KANBC produces the smallest OMA STDV and RMSE for both channels. For the Typhoon In-Fa case examined here, KANBC yields the smallest track errors among the experiments. In particular, KAN-based BC reduces the anomalously strong positive 500 hPa geopotential height increments over the continental high pressure and western North Pacific subtropical high pressure. The resulting initial conditions improve the environmental steering flow and reduce the northwestward track bias. Channel sensitivity experiments were further conducted for water-vapor-sensitive channels 9 and 10 of the FY-4A AGRI using the KAN-based BC method. The results show that single-channel assimilation performs better than dual-channel assimilation. Assimilating channel 10 alone produces the smallest track errors and lower analysis RMSEs relative to ERA5 than assimilating both channels.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Detecting vehicles automatically in high-altitude aerial telemetry remains problematic due to severe scale fluctuations, the erasure of sub-10-pixel targets, and inconsistent annotation formats across different sensors. Standard deep convolutional neural networks depend on strided convolutions and pooling layers that prematurely strip localized pixel details in the opening backbone stages. This used to make permanent feature degradation before the information reaches the subsequent feature fusion neck. To remove these architectural limitations, this research work presents a Multi-Domain Cross-Sensing Framework which is built upon an entirely modified YOLOv26 detector. By replacing the erstwhile standard, lossy Layer 0 intake with a non-destructive Space-to-Depth (SPD) transformation, the spatial sub-matrices are shifted directly into the channel depth, leading to the preservation of total pixel density without any kind of down-sampling distortion. For controlling this expanded feature space and to stabilize the gradient propagation across entire 2400 × 2400 pixel inputs, the model training is driven by a Matrix Momentum Stochastic Gradient Descent (MuSGD) optimizer along with parallel Newton–Schulz polar verification loops. In addition to this, to bridge the cross-domain discrepancies among various ground-level, low-altitude, mid-altitude, and high-altitude recordings, a dynamic coordinate normalization module directly maps data into a single 13-class categorization, which facilitates scale-agnostic interleaved training across the entire COCO, UAV-DT, VisDrone, and piNEUMA datasets. After 143 epochs of continuous training (≈882.6 h/37 days) on an NVIDIA A100 (80 GiB) GPU, the proposed SPD-YOLOv26 model achieved superior sequence-disjoint results on the isolated piNEUMA validation split (𝒟pineuma_val) with higher Precision, Recall, mAP50, and mAP50–95 over default YOLOv26 baselines, simultaneously processing frames at real time speeds of 37 FPS. Overall, these findings confirm that combining lossless intake operations with spectrally bounded gradient updates yields an effective, scale-invariant strategy for wide-area aerial traffic monitoring and intelligent transportation systems.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Daguan County, in the Wumeng Mountains of Yunnan Province, Southwest China, is characterized by complex tectonics, steep terrain and highly seasonal rainfall, and is among the counties most severely affected by geological hazards in the province. This study applies distributed-scatterer time-series InSAR (DS-InSAR) to ascending and descending SAR imagery acquired between March 2024 and July 2026 by the L-band LuTan-1 (LT-1) constellation and by C-band Sentinel-1, deriving wide-area line-of-sight (LOS) velocity fields and deformation time series over a monitoring area of about 6.44 km2 around the county seat. Ascending–descending decomposition and singular spectrum analysis (SSA) separate the deformation at three deforming sites into trend, periodic and residual components, and cross-correlation, lag-response, cumulative regression and wavelet coherence analyses are used to test the temporal association between deformation and meteorological and other external variables. LT-1 shows a clear advantage in vegetated terrain: the ascending stack yields 4.20×104 coherent points per km2, a 23.74-fold density (2274.0% increase) relative to Sentinel-1 and a 42.22-fold density (4122.3%) within vegetated areas, while the descending stack yields 2.47×104 points per km2 (10.55-fold, 954.6%; 22.87-fold, 2186.9% in vegetated areas). Because the two stacks also differ in incidence and azimuth angle, acquisition dates, repeat cycle and processing thresholds, this gain is reported as an operational advantage of the L-band mission rather than as an isolated wavelength effect. At significant-deformation points the two missions agree with correlation coefficients of 0.674 and 0.623 and root-mean-square errors of 15.32 and 2.72 mm/yr; this is moderate agreement, and the ascending comparison rests on only 49 matched pairs. Three clusters of significant deformation (H01–H03) were delineated for |v|≥10 mm/yr. Two-dimensional decomposition indicates that the resolved motion is dominated by east–west horizontal displacement (mean rates of −18.48, −9.24 and −18.21 mm/yr, peak of −46.66 mm/yr at H03), with horizontal-to-vertical trend ratios of 5.30, 1.79 and 4.16; because the north–south component is not resolved, these ratios describe an apparent horizontal dominance whose magnitude depends on the decomposition assumption. SSA reveals cumulative east–west trend displacements of −61.7 mm at H01, −41.8 mm at H02 and −90.3 mm at H03, where the trend rate increases to −44.5 mm/yr. At H03 the periodic amplitude of 30.46 mm reproduces most of the apparent rebound present in the raw series, so the rebound should not be read as stabilization; under the adopted decomposition it does not require a reversal of the trend. The east–west periodic component is associated with maximum air temperature (r = 0.364, 0.439 and 0.466) but only weakly with rainfall at zero lag. Following the M 3.9 earthquake of 12 December 2025, trend rates at H01 and H03 increased briefly, and the acceleration at H01 and H02 coincides in time with the period of most intensive urban construction; these coincidences are consistent with, but do not demonstrate, seismic and engineering triggering.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Remote sensing image-to-online map translation (RSMT) represents a critical research frontier in geographic information processing. With the rapid development of deep learning, generative models have attracted increasing attention for automatic map generation. However, existing methods incur substantial computational overhead when modeling global contextual information. Recently, Mamba has been widely adopted in remote sensing for capturing global dependencies with linear complexity. However, naively applying its sequential scanning mechanism to RSMT tends to disrupt local spatial relationships essential to map generation. In this study, we propose HiMambaMap, a Hilbert-scanning Mamba diffusion network for RSMT. Specifically, it is built upon a Brownian bridge diffusion model, where the proposed denoiser combines a Convolutional Neural Network (CNN) encoder for local spatial feature extraction and a Mamba decoder for efficient global dependency modeling. To further improve the spatial consistency of the generated maps, we introduce a Multi-Scale Feature Fusion (MSFF) module into the denoiser for enhanced multi-scale feature fusion and incorporate a four-directional Hilbert scanning strategy into the Mamba decoder to better preserve spatial locality. Extensive experiments on two widely used datasets demonstrate that HiMambaMap achieves competitive generation quality and pixel-level color consistency, while maintaining the lowest parameter count and computational complexity among several state-of-the-art methods.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The buttressing effect of landfast sea ice is crucial for the stability of Antarctic outlet glaciers. This study investigated spatiotemporal variations in flow velocity and ice thickness at the Shirase Glacier, East Antarctica, from 2016 to 2025 using ALOS-2/PALSAR-2 and CryoSat-2/SIRAL data. Flow velocity increased, and ice thickness decreased downstream, exhibiting a significant negative correlation (r = −0.66, p < 0.05). Notably, during periods of unstable landfast ice (2017–2018 and 2023–2025), substantial ice flow acceleration occurred in the downstream ice tongue. Furthermore, basal melt rates calculated using the Eulerian flux divergence method revealed high melting (16–20 m a−1) near the grounding line, decreasing to 2–4 m a−1 mid-stream, and increasing to 4–8 m a−1 near the ice front. This distribution is consistent with the subglacial oceanic vertical thermal structure. These results quantitatively demonstrate the significant control of both landfast ice stability and subglacial ocean circulation on the spatiotemporal dynamics of the Shirase Glacier.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Joint quantification of glacier terminus change and adjacent secondary landslide evolution can be challenging when multi-temporal UAV datasets lack a consistent absolute spatial reference, thereby limiting robust assessment of their stage-dependent covariation. This study investigated the Hailuogou glacier terminus and the Laoguanjingtai (LGJT) landslide using six UAV photogrammetric surveys acquired between 2018 and 2022 and synchronous airborne light detection and ranging (LiDAR) data from 2022. LiDAR reference constraints and elevation-residual assessment over relatively stable terrain supported multi-temporal digital elevation model (DEM) differencing. The glacier terminus area decreased from 2.63×105m2 to 1.43×105m2, representing a reduction of 45.5%. From July 2020 to August 2022, the glacier volume ablation rate reached 1.00×106m3a−1. Meanwhile, the LGJT landslide shifted from deposition-dominated adjustment from 2018 to 2019 to erosion-dominated material export after July 2020, when its erosion rate reached 3.73×105m3a−1 and its net volume change rate decreased to −3.39×105m3a−1. Annualized rates across five unequal intervals showed positive covariation between glacier ablation and landslide erosion and negative covariation with net landslide volume change. These exploratory associations, together with the spatial concentration of elevation change near the glacier–landslide margin, are consistent with a geomorphic linkage. The LiDAR-constrained UAV workflow provides a practical basis for integrated monitoring of glacier retreat and adjacent sediment redistribution.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Transformer-based deep networks have gained prominence in Polarimetric Synthetic Aperture Radar (PolSAR) image classification due to their strong capability for modeling long-range dependencies. However, existing Transformer-based methods fail to reveal the polarimetric scattering characteristics and their spatial interactions in classification decisions, which leads to limited interpretability and reduced reliability of the models. To address this challenge, this paper proposes an interpretability analysis framework based on attribution inference for Transformer-based PolSAR image classification. Specifically, an attribution inference module is first constructed by integrating structural dependency relationships with gradient-based saliency information, enabling explicit simulation of polarimetric scattering features and their interactions within the model. To enhance the interpretability of PolSAR classification models, we further conduct a full-chain analysis of the Transformer’s internal structure based on the attribution inference matrix, rather than relying on qualitative visualization from a single attention map. This integrated approach enables precise localization of critical attention heads and information flows, while simultaneously identifying discriminative regions that exert a decisive influence on the prediction results. To validate the proposed method, quantitative and qualitative experiments are performed on three PolSAR image classification datasets, which clearly demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed interpretability framework.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Small-object detection in UAV imagery is difficult because targets occupy only a few pixels amid cluttered backgrounds. Although high-resolution features preserve fragile spatial cues, dense contextual interaction over these features is computationally expensive, while multiscale fusion can weaken or misalign local semantic responses. We propose SSC-DETR, a real-time end-to-end detector that addresses these challenges through selective contextual interaction before fusion and selective semantic calibration afterward. Saliency-Guided Sparse Attention (SGSA) learns a spatial selection prior—a saliency map explicitly supervised by Gaussian targets derived from ground-truth bounding boxes—and uses it to select object-relevant high-resolution tokens for sparse self-attention while retaining dense local features elsewhere. Signed Sparse Semantic Calibration (SSSC) independently selects a smaller set of post-fusion positions and injects spatially aligned, bounded signed semantic residuals to reinforce useful evidence or suppress inconsistent responses. Haar-DWT downsampling and a scale-calibrated GIoU–GCD localization objective complement the two modules. Experiments on VisDrone and AI-TOD-v2 demonstrate improvements with ResNet-18 and ResNet-50. With ResNet-50, SSC-DETR increases AP from 29.6% to 35.2% on VisDrone and from 25.5% to 28.9% on AI-TOD-v2 while retaining real-time inference.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Internal solitary waves (ISWs) play an important role in ocean energy transport and mixing, yet their elongated, curved crest lines and low contrast make automated extraction from satellite imagery challenging. To address these challenges, this study proposes YOLO11-ISW, an improved YOLO11-based framework for joint ISW detection and pixel-level segmentation. Dynamic Snake Convolution (DSConv) is introduced to enhance the representation of curved crest-line structures, while the Convolutional Block Attention Module (CBAM) strengthens responses to low-contrast ISW features. A MODIS-based ISW dataset from the northern South China Sea was constructed for model training and evaluation. YOLO11-ISW achieved detection Precision, Recall, and F1-score values of 92.01%, 80.73%, and 86.00%, respectively, and segmentation values of 93.14%, 82.60%, and 87.55%, respectively. The model achieved the highest detection and segmentation F1-scores among the evaluated methods. The segmentation results were further georeferenced to extract ISW crest-line locations, and the framework was applied to MODIS imagery from 2011 to 2020 to investigate satellite-observed ISW spatiotemporal variability. The detected ISWs exhibited pronounced spatial clustering, strong spring–summer seasonality, and clear lunar-cycle modulation, consistent with previous observations in the northern South China Sea. These results demonstrate the potential of YOLO11-ISW for automated, long-term, and large-scale monitoring of ISWs using satellite remote sensing imagery.

Abstract (英文): High-resolution imaging spectroscopy has enabled the detection and quantification of methane emissions from individual facilities, providing valuable information for identifying large emission sources and evaluating emission inventories. This review synthesizes research on broad-spectrum imaging spectrometers through an end-to-end framework linking spectral characterization, methane enhancement retrieval, plume segmentation and emission-rate estimation. We first review atmospheric radiative transfer and spectral characterization, followed by methane enhancement retrieval methods, including IMAP-DOAS, matched filters, and their variants. We then discuss the identification of methane plumes, with particular attention to false-positive suppression and recent developments in automated plume segmentation. Emission-rate estimation methods, including Gaussian plume inversion, cross-sectional flux, integrated mass enhancement, and machine learning approaches, are compared in terms of their underlying assumptions and sources of uncertainty. We also examine the probability of detection and emission persistence, which are essential for interpreting nondetections and estimating emissions from repeated observations. Applications across oil and gas infrastructure, coal mines, landfills, offshore facilities, and accidental releases demonstrate the capability of imaging spectroscopy to identify major emission sources and reveal their spatial and temporal variability. Continued advances in spectral calibration, methane enhancement retrieval, automated plume identification, and emission-rate estimation, together with coordinated multisensor observations, will further improve the accuracy, consistency, and temporal coverage of facility-scale methane monitoring.

</details>

Abstract (英文): A rapid and nondestructive assessment of photosynthetic capacity is valuable for physiological phenotyping and has potential applications in precision crop management. Hyperspectral sensing combined with machine learning provides an efficient approach for estimating leaf physiological traits, yet most existing methods rely on single observations and rarely exploit temporal information contained in sequential measurements. In this study, we developed a trajectory-informed hyperspectral modeling framework for estimating the maximum Rubisco carboxylation rate standardized to 25 °C (Vcmax,25), a key indicator of leaf photosynthetic capacity. The analysis comprised 408 date-level observations derived from 1149 eligible leaf-level measurements from 32 species across 10 sites, organized into intra-seasonal spectral sequences. Conventional snapshot models based on partial least squares regression and support vector regression (SVR) were compared with temporal SVR models incorporating day of year, principal component analysis (PCA)-derived descriptors of recent spectral trajectories, or both. Incorporating spectral trajectory information produced the best performance (R2 = 0.806, RMSE = 15.244 μmol m−2 s−1), compared with R2 = 0.740 and RMSE = 17.649 μmol m−2 s−1 for the snapshot SVR model. Day of year alone yielded only a limited improvement and did not further improve the trajectory-based model. A temporal feature misalignment experiment reduced mean R2 to 0.749 and increased mean RMSE to 17.310 μmol m−2 s−1, indicating that the predictive gain depended on preserving the correspondence between each observation and its temporal context rather than simply increasing predictor dimensionality. SHAP analysis further showed that instantaneous spectral information, particularly in the red-edge and shortwave-infrared regions, remained the dominant source of prediction, while temporal descriptors provided complementary information on recent spectral change. These results indicate that incorporating temporal spectral trajectories can extend conventional snapshot-based machine learning and provide a useful strategy for physiological phenotyping of leaf photosynthetic capacity across the growing season.

</details>

Abstract (英文): High-precision digital surface models (DSMs) derived from optical stereo satellite data are essential for various applications. However, DSMs generated from high-resolution satellite imagery often contain high-frequency errors associated with calibration inaccuracies and attitude jitter. This study exploits differences in the error characteristics of optical stereo-derived and interferometric synthetic aperture radar (InSAR)-derived DSMs to detect, model, and compensate for these errors. Dense bundle adjustment first corrects low-frequency errors in the optical stereo image pair. Elevation differences between the resulting optical DSM and the InSAR DSM are then analyzed to identify coherent directional error patterns. An object-space compensation model based on median elevation-difference profiles is established, and the elevation corrections are transferred to image space to compensate for the stereo imagery. Experiments using a single Ziyuan-3 02 (ZY-3 02) stereo pair showed reduced stripe patterns associated with residual high-frequency errors. Using the 30 m Copernicus GLO-30 DSM as the elevation reference, the flight-direction median-difference root mean square error (RMSE) decreased from 0.43 to 0.22 m (48.8%). The scan-direction RMSE decreased from 0.51 to 0.25 m (51.0%). Both reductions were relative to the Quadratic adjustment. The image-space residual RMSE calculated from the fitted error curves decreased from 0.132 to 0.032 pixels after compensation. Independent evaluation against ALOS World 3D 30 m (AW3D30) also showed reduced high-frequency systematic distortions. The flight- and scan-direction median-difference RMSEs decreased from 0.58 to 0.35 m (39.7%) and from 0.78 to 0.37 m (52.6%), respectively.

Abstract (英文): Rapid and accurate soil organic carbon (SOC) quantification from UAV hyperspectral data is challenged by spectral redundancy and the limited generalization of individual models. This study proposes an RFE–TOPSIS–stacking method, a hybrid feature selection and stacking ensemble framework for SOC estimation using UAV hyperspectral data (350–1002 nm). Recursive feature elimination (RFE) based on XGBoost was first employed for preliminary band screening, retaining 19 bands. Then, the Technique for Order Preference by Similarity to Ideal Solution (TOPSIS), a multi-criteria decision-making method, was applied to comprehensively evaluate these bands using mutual evaluation, integrating mutual information, entropy, random forest importance, the inverse coefficient of variation of reflectance, and band redundancy, and was then applied to select 12 SOC characteristic bands. A stacking regression model was subsequently constructed using a 1D-CNN, SVR, XGBoost, and PLSR as base learners and random forest as the meta-learner. The framework achieved an R2 of 0.85, an RMSE of 0.96 g/kg, and an RPD of 2.47 for the validation set, outperforming the individual model (1D-CNN: R2 = 0.78, RMSE = 1.05 g/kg, and RPD =2.26). Application to the three study areas revealed SOC contents (P10–P90) of 7.37–9.59, 7.52–11.01, and 9.98–15.43 g/kg; most validation sampling points had absolute residuals < 1 g/kg (max: 1.65 g/kg), and approximately 80% had relative errors within ±10%. The proposed framework provides a methodological basis for UAV-based field-scale SOC estimation and spatial mapping, with potential applications in precision agriculture and soil carbon assessment.

Abstract (英文): This article proposes a fully automated engineering pipeline for extracting surface water from Sentinel-2 satellite imagery using a novel spectral index and unsupervised three-cluster classification. The proposed Water Body Enhancement Index (WBEI) relies exclusively on native 10 m spatial resolution bands (Blue, Green, Red, and Near-Infrared [NIR]), thereby eliminating the geometric degradation caused by Short-Wave Infrared (SWIR) band resampling. To ensure methodological robustness across diverse global aquatic environments, WBEI is validated across six distinct experimental sites located in Italy, Australia, the USA, and Mozambique, utilizing target test fields of no less than 100,000 pixels per scene. Performance is rigorously compared via an identical automated framework against widely used conventional water indices: Automated Water Extraction Index-Shadow version (AWEIsh), Modified Normalized Difference Water Index (MNDWI), Normalized Difference Water Index (NDWI), and Water Ratio Index (WRI). Validation metrics include traditional confusion matrices (overall, producer’s, and user’s accuracy) and the spatial Ratio Index (RI) expressed in meters to precisely evaluate shoreline displacement. Experimental results indicate that the proposed WBEI consistently achieves superior extraction accuracy, with overall accuracy values exceeding 94.9% in all geographic cases, offering a highly reliable, reproducible, and fully automated pipeline managed via a Geographic Information System (GIS).

Abstract (英文): Spatiotemporal fusion techniques can generate remote sensing imagery with both high spatial and temporal resolutions, thereby supporting a wide range of Earth observation applications. Although recent diffusion-based STF methods have shown promising reconstruction performance, generation from Gaussian noise typically requires many iterative sampling steps, resulting in considerable inference cost. To enable efficient few-step reconstruction, we propose WPFM-STF, a wavelet-constrained prior-guided Flow Matching framework for remote sensing spatiotemporal fusion, specifically adapting Flow Matching to the characteristics of STF by formulating reconstruction as a continuous transport process from a learned spatiotemporal prior to the target fine-resolution image. Specifically, a learnable prior generator integrates multi-temporal and multi-resolution observations to produce an initial estimate that already captures scene structure and temporal variation, thereby shortening the effective transport path compared with noise-based generation. Conditional Flow Matching is then employed to learn the prior-to-target velocity field, providing a more flexible and progressive refinement process than one-shot regression while enabling accurate reconstruction with only a few ordinary differential equation evaluations. To further preserve fine spatial structures during few-step transport, a wavelet detail constraint is imposed on the high-frequency components of a residual-induced auxiliary reconstruction derived from the predicted velocity field. Extensive experiments on the CIA, LGC, and Daxing datasets demonstrate that WPFM-STF achieves competitive or superior reconstruction performance with only a few ODE evaluations; ablation studies validate the effectiveness of the learned spatiotemporal prior, prior-guided flow refinement, and wavelet detail constraint; and efficiency analyses further confirm its substantial advantage over iterative diffusion-based STF methods.

</details>

Abstract (英文): This article evaluates the contribution of multi-temporal UAV remote sensing to archaeological prospection in a Dutch grassland context, using the moated castle site of ’t Huis ten Bosch as a detailed case study. Six UAV campaigns, spanning more than four years and deploying thermal, multispectral, RGB and LiDAR sensors, are compared with magnetometry, electromagnetic induction, and coring data, and evaluated against seasonal and atmospheric conditions. The combined dataset corroborates and refines the subsurface record of the primary moat, secondary moat, and central tower and walls, demonstrating that no single prospection technique, applied in isolation, could have produced the same level of structural detail and interpretative confidence. A systematic comparison of anomaly visibility across campaigns shows that detectability varied considerably between campaigns, tracking soil properties, moisture history, vegetation phenology, and mowing regime, with the clearest results tending to coincide with transitional meteorological conditions instead of prolonged extremes, on the evidence of six campaigns. The study highlights both the promise and the site-specific constraints of UAV-based prospection, and identifies methodological limitations related to sensor availability, campaign density, and the use of regional meteorological data. It concludes by outlining some provisional best practices for UAV survey design and by arguing for stratified, multi-site implementations to develop a comparative empirical basis for optimising sensor deployment in archaeological prospection in the Netherlands and beyond.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Long-term monitoring of land use/land cover (LULC) and surface water is challenging in monsoon lake watersheds with limited reference observations. This study constructed annual LULC in the Inle lake watershed, Myanmar, for 2000–2025 using Landsat annual median composites, six reflectance bands and six spectral indices. Random Forest, Support Vector Machine (SVM) and XGBoost were evaluated using spatially grouped internal testing of purposively selected reference samples. SVM achieved the highest 26-year mean internal overall accuracy (71.49%), kappa (0.620) and macro-F1 (71.79%). A separate stratified probability sample of 600 fixed pixels was assessed for 2000, 2010, 2020 and 2025 to estimate map accuracy, class areas and paired net changes within a common 5438.20 km2 domain. Area-weighted overall accuracy ranged from 58.7% to 71.4%, while Built-up user’s accuracy remained low at 12.4–22.9%. Reference-based 2000–2025 changes were Agriculture +450.1 km2 (95% sampling interval: +203.4 to +696.8), Forest −631.5 km2 (−857.3 to −405.7) and Water +42.3 km2 (−23.5 to +108.0). The Water interval included zero, and the reference assessment did not establish a 2020–2025 recovery. Annual trend significance for Agriculture and Built-up changed under assumed classification error correction scenarios. Within the Landsat 8-only period (2013–2025), mapped Forest still declined, whereas mapped Agriculture showed no monotonic trend. Within the fixed open water outline of Inle lake, mapped water declined slowly but consistently (−0.07 km2 yr−1; p < 0.001), the Moebye reservoir showed no trend, and most of the mapped decline occurred in the surrounding sanctuary wetlands. Most of the reference-based agricultural increase and forest loss occurred between 2000 and 2010. The results support agricultural expansion and forest loss but leave watershed-wide surface water change uncertain. Distinguishing raw mapped trajectories from reference-based estimates and sampling uncertainty is essential for interpreting long-term watershed change.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Object detection in remote sensing images (RSIs) suffers severely from small-scale instances, which are inherently constrained by extremely low pixel occupancy, fragile spatial detail retention, and insufficient discriminative feature representation. Global scaling is an intuitive strategy to compensate for the spatial information loss of small targets, yet it inevitably brings significant computational complexity as convolutional networks grow quadratically with the elevation of spatial resolution. In order to alleviate this accuracy–efficiency dilemma, we propose a focus, magnify, detect network, named adaptive region-aware detector (ARADet), tailored for object detection in RSIs. In the focus stage, our region-aware attention (RAA) module generates a Gaussian-constrained attention heatmap to rapidly localize potential small-target regions from the regional attention map. In the magnify stage, we introduce a differentiable affine magnification mechanism with learnable scaling factors. For each focused region, the network adaptively predicts a scaling factor and constructs a center-aligned affine transformation matrix to achieve center-preserving regional magnification. Unlike global upsampling schemes, our module only enhances target-region features and fills them back into the original feature map, thus keeping the overall feature resolution unchanged. The final enhanced features are fed into the detection head for category classification and oriented bounding-box regression. Extensive experiments conducted on two standard aerial object detection benchmarks, including DOTA and DIOR-R, demonstrate that ARADet brings 1.18% mAP improvement over the baseline method, with merely 0.3726M additional parameters. Although the introduced feature amplification yields a moderate rise in FLOPs, ARADet achieves favorable accuracy gains with negligible parameter overhead and surpasses many state-of-the-art oriented detectors.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Dynamic World provides near-real-time 10 m land-cover data, but scene-level classifications are affected by observation conditions and seasonal changes, causing missing valid data and unstable class labels. Using 2020 Dynamic World data, this study evaluated different temporal windows and compositing methods to identify suitable strategies for mapping stable annual land-cover types in China. We compared mode, cumulative-probability, and maximum-confidence composites at monthly, growing-season (April–October), and annual scales. Spatial coverage was assessed, and classification accuracy was evaluated using 14,042 reference samples. Effective observation rates showed clear spatial and temporal differences. Rates were lower in southwestern China, higher in northwestern and northern China, and generally lower in summer. Longer compositing windows significantly reduced data gaps. The monthly scale spatial coverage was 68.78%, while the growing-season and full-year windows increased spatial coverage to 98.52% and 98.57%, respectively. However, full-year coverage gains over the growing-season window were limited. Growing-season mode and cumulative-probability composites outperformed the other strategies, with the highest macro-F1 (64.96%) and OA (76.19%), respectively. However, performance varied by time window and land-cover type, and no method consistently performed best. These results can guide the selection of time windows and compositing methods for regional Dynamic World mapping.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Hyperspectral image (HSI) super-resolution often uses RGB or RGB-like high-resolution guidance, which provides no direct high-resolution observation in the near-infrared (NIR). Keeping the low-resolution HSI spectrally complete, we first add one high-resolution NIR band near 801 nm to RGB. For abundance-Gram reconstruction, this reduces full-spectrum mean per-band RMSE by 92.2% on Chikusei and 75.5% on ICVL. We then replace RGB with only one to four high-resolution narrowbands spanning the visible and NIR. A few strategically distributed bands support strong full-spectrum reconstruction while substantially reducing the high-resolution spectral measurement burden. Using the same sparse observations, abundance-Gram reconstruction is strongly sensitive to wavelength placement, whereas registered HySure-SVD is much less sensitive. A targeted three-seed analysis confirms that the key 549 + 801 nm and four-band spanning designs are highly stable, while some weaker configurations are seed-sensitive. A guide-permutation stress test links the cross-architecture contrast to permutation-invariant global abundance-statistics matching versus registered pixelwise fusion. Sensor-native HySpex Mjolnir imagery reproduces the same crossover. These results support sparse visible-NIR sensing and joint design of high-resolution measurements and reconstruction.

Abstract (英文): Accurate geometric positioning is essential for the quantitative application of synthetic aperture radar (SAR) imagery. Although geometric calibration can effectively reduce systematic positioning errors, the cross-scene transferability of calibration-derived correction parameters remains insufficiently evaluated, particularly under different acquisition conditions. This study investigates the geometric positioning accuracy and parameter transferability of GF-3B/C SAR imagery using observations from the Xilinhot and Lijiang calibration sites. Scene-specific range and azimuth-time corrections were first estimated within the Range–Doppler framework, followed by cross-scene transfer experiments and acquisition-condition sensitivity analyses. The results indicate that scene-specific correction established a high-accuracy baseline, reducing the median planimetric root-mean-square error (RMSE) from 57.50 m to 1.53 m. In cross-scene transfer, the range correction exhibited substantially higher stability than the azimuth-time correction. Range-only transfer yielded a median combined two-dimensional RMSE of 50.98 m, whereas combined transfer yielded 60.51 m, compared with 57.85 m before transfer. Transfer performance was associated with source–target acquisition compatibility, yielding superior accuracy when satellite platform, orbit direction, and Doppler centroid frequency differences were well matched. Furthermore, cross-site transfer demonstrated accuracy comparable to that of same-site transfer under compatible conditions. These findings indicate that parameter transferability depends on both the specific correction component and acquisition compatibility. For GF-3B/C imagery in ground-control-point-free (GCP-free) scenarios, range corrections offer strong potential for direct reuse, whereas azimuth-time corrections require rigorous compatibility screening during source-scene selection.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Low vegetation cover and patchy distribution in arid regions make vegetation spectral signals susceptible to interference from bare-soil backgrounds, while differences in canopy structure among herbaceous vegetation, shrubs, and trees further increase the difficulty of accurate aboveground biomass (AGB) estimation. This study investigated heterogeneous vegetation in the middle and lower plains of the Yarkand River Basin, China, using field-measured AGB, UAV multispectral imagery, and LiDAR point clouds. Spectral (S), canopy height (H), and texture (T) features were derived, and four combinations (S, S + H, S + T, and S + H + T) were evaluated with SVR, GPR, XGBoost, and RF under repeated nested five-fold cross-validation. The results showed that the optimal texture window size varied among models, with the 7 × 7 pixel window providing a reasonable balance between prediction accuracy and stability. Adding canopy height to spectral-only models increased R2 by 0.282–0.455 and reduced RMSE by 28.2–41.9%, exceeding the incremental contribution of texture. S + H + T achieved the best overall performance (R2 = 0.643–0.806; RMSE = 1.189–1.629 kg·m−2). XGBoost yielded the highest mean accuracy, whereas RF was more stable. SHAP analysis attributed 63.6%, 30.2%, and 6.2% of the overall contribution to canopy height, spectral, and texture features, respectively. Overall, multisource feature integration improved AGB estimation, with canopy height providing the main incremental information; however, accurately retrieving canopy height for low-stature vegetation in arid regions remains a key challenge for further improving estimation reliability.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Surface soil moisture (SSM) regulates land–atmosphere water and energy exchanges over the Tibetan Plateau (TP); yet, passive microwave SSM products, such as those from the Soil Moisture Active Passive (SMAP) mission, are limited to coarse spatial resolutions of tens of kilometers. Although various downscaling approaches have been developed using radar backscatter, thermal infrared observations, and optical–thermal indicators, the value of shortwave infrared (SWIR) reflectance—whose moisture-dependent absorption characteristics offer fine-scale soil moisture information—remains insufficiently assessed across contrasting environments. To address this gap, we developed a 1 km SSM product for 2017–2020 by downscaling 36 km MCCA-SMAP estimates over the TP, combining the normalized shortwave difference soil moisture index (NSDSI) derived from MODIS reflectance with a first-order Taylor expansion and daily sensitivity calibration. The product was evaluated against ground observations from five monitoring networks spanning diverse climatic and land surface conditions using spatial, temporal, regional-scale, and spatiotemporal comparison frameworks, and was benchmarked against three published 1 km downscaled products based on radar backscatter, diurnal temperature amplitude, and soil evaporation efficiency, respectively. Compared with the original MCCA-SMAP SSM, the spatial regression slopes of the NSDSI-based downscaled product moved closer to unity at all five networks, with spatial correlations increasing at four networks particularly at SMN-WDL (from −0.04 to 0.59) and Shiquanhe (from 0.12 to 0.44), although the near-unity slopes at Shiquanhe were sensitive to unusually high estimates at SQ11. The NSDSI-based downscaled product showed lower mean station-level temporal correlations across the five networks, whereas regional-scale temporal correlations remained nearly unchanged. Relative to the existing 1 km products, the NSDSI-based product had spatial slopes closer to unity at all networks except Maqu, although correlation and error rankings varied among networks. At Maqu, where the vegetation cover is relatively high, the NSDSI-based downscaled SSM had spatial correlations below 0.2 and exhibited lower correlations and a higher ubRMSD than the radar- and thermal inertia-based downscaled products. The resulting downscaled SSM maps preserved the broad west-to-east moisture gradient and showed finer spatial detail. These multi-network results identify improved spatial pattern representation as the principal benefit of the evaluated SWIR-assisted downscaling approach, while demonstrating that gains in spatial agreement do not necessarily improve temporal performance or reduce ubRMSD.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Comprehensive spatial inventories of irrigation channels inform water-resource management, hydrological modeling, and infrastructure planning, yet remain unavailable for many irrigated regions due to the high cost of manual mapping. Here we present a novel approach for irrigation channel mapping that integrates very high-resolution (0.3–0.5 m) WorldView satellite imagery, 1 m LiDAR-derived digital elevation model data, and ancillary land cover and irrigation datasets. The approach employs a U-Net convolutional neural network and a Hessian filter to identify potential surface-water features from optical imagery, while geomorphon classification identifies potential channel locations from digital elevation model data. A constrained dilation algorithm expands high-confidence channel detections along topographically favorable paths, and an XGBoost classifier distinguishes irrigation channels from natural surface-water features and false positives. Applied to a 569 km2 study area in the San Luis Valley, Colorado, the approach mapped 552 linear kilometers of irrigation channels—18% more than the U.S. Geological Survey National Hydrography Dataset Plus High Resolution and 35% more than the Colorado Division of Water Resources inventory. Object-based accuracy assessment indicated that 92.3% of mapped channel segments were definite or possible irrigation channels, with only 7.7% representing false positives. Among channel segments not appearing in existing datasets, 76% were judged to be definite (43%) or possible (33%) irrigation channels either missed by previous mapping efforts or constructed after the creation of those datasets. While recall (the proportion of reference channel pixels that were correctly mapped) remained limited (0.28), the approach demonstrates potential for rapidly generating spatially explicit channel inventories that provide a starting point for manual refinement: false positives can be removed quickly, whereas adding missed channels requires more substantial effort.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Sea surface temperature (SST) exerts a critical influence on diverse physical and biological processes within the marine environment. Nevertheless, accurate short-term SST forecasting still remains challenging. Current deep learning (DL) models have two key limitations: first, the convolutional neural network (CNN)-based methods fail to capture global spatiotemporal dependencies; second, Transformer-based approaches suffer from relatively high computational costs. To address these issues, this study introduces a VMamba–CNN fusion network (VMCF), a dual-branch architecture designed to effectively characterize the multiscale temporal and spatial variability of SST. The VMCF combines the powerful local feature extraction capability of CNNs with the efficient linear-computability global spatiotemporal representation of VMamba, further strengthened via channel-wise feature fusion. Extensive experiments on the OISST dataset demonstrate significant performance improvements across all evaluation metrics, clearly validating the superiority of VMCF in SST forecasting. Specifically, it achieves root-mean-square error (RMSE) values ranging from 0.17 °C to 0.51 °C across 1–10-day lead times, outperforming five state-of-the-art DL models.

Abstract (英文): Historical aerial photographs (HAPs) provide unique high-resolution records of past landscapes and are an important source for long-term administrative, environmental, archaeological, and spatial research. However, the large-scale transformation of national analogue archives into geometrically consistent geospatial datasets remains challenging because of heterogeneous image quality, incomplete metadata, and varying camera systems. This paper presents the georeferencing of 83,467 analogue aerial photographs covering Slovenia, acquired between 1953 and 2005. The archive was processed within 18 months, combining manual, semi-automatic, and automated procedures with systematic quality control. Preprocessing included image inspection, radiometric enhancement, fiducial mark extraction, frame masking, and preparation of camera calibration information, with the oldest imagery requiring the greatest manual intervention. Aerial triangulation was performed using a Structure from Motion-based workflow in Agisoft Metashape, supplemented by custom Python scripts. The resulting dataset comprises 45 annual orthophoto mosaics with a spatial resolution of 0.5 m. The resulting orthomosaics achieved accuracies (mean RMSE values) ranging from 1.46 to 3.26 m. The project demonstrates that large national archives of heterogeneous HAPs can be transformed into consistent, analysis-ready geospatial datasets. It also represents one of the few reported national-scale efforts to systematically georeference an archive of HAPs spanning more than five decades.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The Water Cloud Model (WCM) is the most widely used vegetation-scattering correction framework for microwave soil-moisture (SM) remote sensing, yet its cross-site generalization ability remains poorly understood. This study compares WCM-based random forest models across four validation networks spanning a landscape-heterogeneity gradient (REMEDHUS, SMOSMANIA, Little Washita, and OzNet; station-mean coefficient of variation (CV) of 0.18–0.67), under both within-station random-split and leave-one-station-out (LOSO) validation. The WCM-decoupled feature yields no significant accuracy gain under random validation (Wilcoxon p ≥ 0.168), and all models fall below a monthly climatology baseline under LOSO, consistent with a generalization failure that is more plausibly associated with station-level distributional shift than with vegetation-correction error. LOSO R2 decreases monotonically with landscape heterogeneity across the four networks, but at the subnetwork level it is robust only at the most heterogeneous network (REMEDHUS); in homogeneous landscapes, temporal transferability appears more important. A data-leakage artifact is identified and corrected in anomaly-based modeling, substantially reducing apparent gains at two of the four sites. A target-station-mean correction calibrated from only the earliest 10% of a new station’s observations lifts R2 above the climatology baseline at the three most heterogeneous networks, suggesting that much of the failure is a correctable station-baseline shift, although other factors cannot be excluded. These findings provide supporting evidence on the applicability boundary of WCM–machine-learning frameworks for network-scale soil moisture retrieval, based on the four networks examined.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Two U.S. National Estuarine Research Reserves (NERRs), Rookery Bay (Florida) and Jobos Bay (Puerto Rico), experienced hurricanes in September 2017 (Irma and Maria) and 2022 (Ian and Fiona). We quantified mangrove canopy disturbance and recovery using pixel-wise annual land-cover trajectories from 2 m WorldView™ imagery (2017–2024) and monthly Sentinel-2 and Landsat Normalized Difference Vegetation Index (NDVI) time series (2016–2025). Mangrove target areas (MTAs) comprised pixels classified as mangrove in the pre-storm 2017 map and consistent coverage across the subsequent available maps. In 2018, mapped mangrove cover declined to 47% in the southeastern Rookery Bay MTA (sRB-MTA) and 59% in the Jobos Bay MTA (JB-MTA), with most disturbed pixels reclassified as soil. Intermediate mapped classes represented spectral and surface-condition changes rather than discrete ecological states. Despite greater initial loss, mapped mangrove cover reached 90% in the sRB-MTA by 2023, compared with 79% in the JB-MTA in 2023 and 83% in 2024. Recovery was spatially heterogeneous: pixels initially reclassified as mixed low-relief vegetation showed higher recovery percentages by 2023 (sRB-MTA: 92%; JB-MTA: 81%) than those reclassified as coastal water (48% and 23%, respectively). Persistent non-recovery of mangrove class coincided with pre-existing hydrologic stressors in both regions, and unauthorized coastal development in Jobos Bay NERR. Areas that subsequently returned to the mangrove class showed a sharp post-storm NDVI decline, followed by an initial rapid rebound and gradual recovery toward climatological greenness and seasonality by mid-2022. NDVI recovery began earlier in Jobos Bay (November 2017) than in southern Rookery Bay (June 2018). No widespread canopy response was detected following the 2022 storms, consistent with weaker local wind exposure but not excluding other ecological effects. Together, land-cover trajectories and the NDVI resolved complementary canopy recovery patterns without establishing restoration of pre-storm forest structure or ecosystem function.

</details>

However, indicator-level diagnosis remains constrained by the lack of a coherent and unified assessment framework. Focusing on 11 coastal provinces of China, this study constructs a localized SDG 14-aligned indicator system based on the pressure-state-response (PSR) framework, evaluates indicators in terms of score, interaction, and network centrality, and integrates these components into a multi-criteria analysis (MCA) framework to identify focal indicators for coastal sustainable development. The results show that the share of tertiary marine industries is identified as a high-scoring positive-interaction central indicator, while fishing vessel tonnage per capacity unit is identified as a high-scoring trade-off-related central indicator. In addition, fishery economic output per GDP is identified as a low-scoring-trade-off-related central indicator. These findings suggest that future coastal development should place greater emphasis on marine industrial upgrading, fishing-capacity regulation, and the coordinated improvement of fishery economic benefits and sustainability performance, so as to create more favorable conditions for strengthening synergistic development. The proposed framework supports indicator-level diagnosis and differentiated management attention for localized coastal sustainable development.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The start of season (SOS) is widely used to assess forest phenological responses to climate change, but how well it represents subsequent canopy greening remains unclear. Using GIMMS NDVI3g and climate data for 1983–2014, we examined greenness-defined SOS and the start of canopy maturity (SOM) across deciduous needleleaf (DNF), mixed (MF), and deciduous broadleaf (DBF) forests in northern China, and assessed the additional explanatory information provided by seasonal climate beyond SOS. After detrending, SOS and SOM were positively correlated in all three forest types (r = 0.57–0.80, all p < 0.01). However, long-term trend estimates showed regional differences: SOS and SOM tended to advance in DNF and DBF, whereas SOS tended to advance and SOM to be delayed in MF. SOS alone accounted for 34.7–58.3% of the interannual variation in SOM; seasonal climate explained additional SOM variation in DBF after accounting for SOS. Thus, although spring green-up onset and later canopy greening are significantly correlated at the interannual scale, they do not necessarily follow parallel long-term trajectories. Incorporating both spring onset and subsequent canopy greening, together with their seasonal climate associations, into phenological models may improve projections of forest spring development under climate change.

</details>

Abstract (英文): For the maritime over-the-horizon (OTH) propagation of electromagnetic (EM) waves in both C-band and X-band, evaporation ducts (EDs) and troposcatter are two dominant mechanisms, and they are significantly influenced by near-surface maritime atmospheric conditions. Most of the existing research treats ED and troposcatter as distinct mechanisms. However, one or multiple transitions will occur between them within one day, and there is no related research on the accurate identification of EDs and troposcatter. Therefore, we propose an identification method of OTH propagation mechanisms in maritime atmospheric environments based on measured path losses (PLs) in the northern South China Sea (SCS). Firstly, the method of exponentially weighted moving average (EWMA) is utilized to smooth the measured PLs. Secondly, the identification criterion and corresponding algorithm are proposed to identify the troposcatter mechanism. Thirdly, a Gaussian mixture model (GMM) combined with the expectation maximization algorithm are utilized to identify the ED mechanism and transition states. The performance of the proposed method is sufficiently validated via the measured PLs obtained from the northern SCS. The results show that compared with the intuitive method based on the forecasted evaporation duct height (EDH), the proposed method provides quasi-real-time and more reliable identification of maritime OTH propagation mechanisms.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Conventional water vapor–precipitation studies rely on column-integrated or single-site observations, which cannot capture the three-dimensional (3D) structure of moisture aggregation and its role in precipitation triggering in complex terrain where convective, frontal, and orographic rainfall coexist. Using Ningxia Continuously Operating Reference Station (NXCORS) Global Navigation Satellite System (GNSS) observations (2018–2021), we develop a 3D water vapor tomography model (5 km × 6 km × 1 km computational grid; 5 min retrieval interval), validated against independent radiosonde and ERA5 data (r = 0.953/0.881; RMSE = 1.998/1.949 g m−3). We propose 3D Water Vapor Preferential Zones (WVPZs) and an object-based framework using stratified dual-percentile thresholds (99%/97%) and 3D connected-component tracking to identify, track, and match WVPZs with rainfall regions under temporal (≤6 h prior) and spatial (≤100 km centroid distance) constraints. Across 16 events, WVPZs were present in 94.97% of 5 min time steps. In 416 matched pairs, WVPZs typically formed 2–6 h before rainfall, with a mean precursor time lag of 231.5 min at onset. Mean centroid distances were 52–55 km (mean minimum centroid distance: 39.4 km). By rainfall type, convective rainfall had the shortest precursor time lag (220.6 min) but largest distance (55.7 km); frontal rainfall showed the tightest coupling (49.1 km); orographic rainfall had the longest precursor time lag (254.0 min). Sample-derived precursor-lag and spatial-proximity ranges are 3–5 h and 30–60 km. This framework shifts moisture diagnosis from continuous fields to discrete, trackable water vapor objects, providing a quantitative 3D indicator for characterizing pre-precipitation moisture organization and supporting precipitation monitoring in complex terrain.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Clouds are core components of the global climate system and hydrological cycle, and high-precision cloud detection is a key prerequisite for quantitative optical remote sensing applications. Traditional threshold methods often miss thin clouds and misclassify high-albedo surfaces; existing pixel-level models ignore spatial context, causing class imbalance and detection bias. This study proposes the graph attention convolutional network (GACN) for daytime and nighttime cloud detection across land cover types using Advanced Himawari Imager (AHI) data. It adopts a dual-branch architecture to extract spatial correlations and multi-scale spectral features, paired with an adaptive fusion gate and multi-objective joint loss to balance detection performance. The graph attention branch builds dynamic spatial–spectral neighbor graphs to resolve spectral confusion between thin clouds and bright underlying surfaces. The proposed metric-constrained joint loss is also portable to other network architectures to mitigate class imbalance in pixel-level remote sensing classification. Trained on 2 km Himawari-8/9 AHI data with CALIPSO lidar labels, GACN achieves a TSS of 75.18% (daytime) and 71.57% (nighttime) with DR below 7%, outperforming seven baselines and two official AHI cloud mask variants. It provides an effective solution for high-accuracy AHI cloud mask generation and is more suited for regional-scale applications or offline post hoc scientific processing.

Abstract (英文): This study examined whether joint vegetation-greenness and moisture-sensitive spectral trends across four standardized calendar windows form spatially reproducible archetypes in Aseer, Saudi Arabia, during 2015–2025, and how those archetypes relate to terrain and land cover. The normalized difference vegetation index (NDVI) and normalized difference moisture index (NDMI) were characterized via Kendall’s tau and Sen’s slope across four standardized three-month calendar windows. Gaussian mixture models (GMMs) with alternative component numbers and covariance structures were evaluated via spatial-block resampling, emphasizing geographic reproducibility rather than likelihood-based fit alone. Although higher-component models achieved more favorable likelihood criteria, the five-component diagonal GMM was substantially more reproducible, achieving a median adjusted Rand index of 0.912, normalized mutual information of 0.890, and centroid cosine similarity of 0.994. The resulting archetypes captured concordant positive, vegetation–moisture discordant, calendar-window-dependent, and weak or mixed trajectories. Terrain and land cover showed moderate environmental separability (balanced accuracy = 0.405), with elevation, local relief, and slope contributing most strongly. These findings reveal contrasting vegetation–moisture trend patterns across Aseer, with directional discordance concentrated in northern and northeastern interiors and positive or calendar-window-dependent trajectories prominent in western and southwestern mountain sectors. Spatial reproducibility provides a complementary criterion for selecting unsupervised classifications that support geographically meaningful environmental analysis.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate extraction of surface water is of great significance for water resource management, ecological conservation, and flood disaster monitoring. However, water bodies in high-resolution remote sensing imagery are often obscured by building shadows, vegetation, complex backgrounds, or spectral confusion. Consequently, traditional semantic segmentation methods frequently struggle with global feature modeling and the preservation of boundary details. To address these challenges, this paper proposes MFM-TransUNet, a remote sensing water extraction network based on multi-scale feature modeling. The network employs an encoder–feature modeling–decoder architecture. A Ghost-SE encoder is utilized to extract multi-scale hierarchical features, while a High-Performance Sequence Modeling Module (HPSM) is introduced to aggregate local contextual information from high-level features through depthwise 1D convolutions, with a feed-forward network enhancing nonlinear feature representations along the channel dimension. During the decoding phase, a learnable weight-fusion strategy is applied to balance the contributions of features from different levels, and an Edge-enhanced Feature Attention (EFA) is incorporated to strengthen boundary-related information within the decoded features. The proposed method was evaluated in terms of segmentation accuracy, module ablation, and cross-dataset performance. Experiments on the GID dataset demonstrate that MFM-TransUNet achieves an Overall Accuracy (OA) of 96.27%, a Mean Intersection over Union (Mean IoU) of 89.24%, an F1-Score of 94.20%, and a Boundary IoU of 63.52%, showing competitive performance compared with several mainstream baseline models. Ablation studies evaluate the contributions of the key modules, while experiments on the LJ-Water and Water-CD datasets assess the model’s performance across different datasets. These results suggest that MFM-TransUNet achieves a favorable trade-off between segmentation accuracy, boundary-related performance, and computational cost, providing a potential approach for surface water extraction in complex remote sensing scenarios.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The Geostationary Ocean Color Imager-II (GOCI-II) provides high-frequency multispectral observations over East Asia, offering considerable potential for terrestrial monitoring. Surface reflectance (SR) is a fundamental land product that represents surface spectral properties after accounting for atmospheric effects and serves as a primary input to downstream products such as surface albedo and vegetation indices. Consequently, uncertainties in SR retrieval can propagate through the processing chain and affect the accuracy and consistency of these derived land products. However, the original GOCI-II land SR algorithm was developed before launch using GOCI proxy data and employed a temporal minimum-reflectance selection scheme, limiting its ability to fully account for the actual spectral, geometric, and atmospheric characteristics of GOCI-II observations. In this study, we developed and evaluated an improved GOCI-II-specific atmospheric correction framework based on a 6SV lookup table (LUT) constructed for all 12 reflective bands. The revised retrieval incorporates pixel-level observation geometry, aerosol optical depth, water vapor, ozone, and terrain elevation and produces observation-specific clear-sky SR without temporal minimum compositing. The original and improved land products were evaluated using all 12 Local Area slots throughout 2023. SR was validated against MODIS MCD19A1, broadband albedo against MODIS MCD43A3, and NDVI and EVI against MODIS MOD13Q1 and GK2A/AMI products. For SR, the largest improvements occurred at shorter wavelengths: RMSE decreased from 0.271 to 0.042 at 412 nm, from 0.196 to 0.032 at 443 nm, and from 0.134 to 0.036 at 490 nm. Improvements were also observed at longer wavelengths, and a common-pixel analysis confirmed that the reductions in bias and RMSE persisted when the original and improved products were evaluated using an identical set of valid pixels. The revised processing also improved the downstream land products. The RMSE of broadband black-sky albedo decreased from 0.037 to 0.025, while that of white-sky albedo decreased from 0.039 to 0.035. For vegetation indices, NDVI RMSE decreased from 0.403 to 0.142 against MODIS MOD13Q1 and from 0.342 to 0.100 against GK2A/AMI, while EVI RMSE decreased from 0.274 to 0.111 and from 0.268 to 0.071, respectively. These results demonstrate that sensor-specific post-launch atmospheric correction substantially improves GOCI-II SR and that enhanced accuracy of this fundamental upstream product contributes to greater consistency and accuracy throughout the downstream GOCI-II land-product chain.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Northern China and Mongolia are among the core regions most significantly affected by desertification globally. Desertification in these areas not only triggers frequent transboundary dust storms but also severely threatens the ecological security and sustainable development of both countries and the Northeast Asian region. It has become a key bottleneck for achieving the regional SDG 15.3 target of land degradation neutrality. However, existing large-scale desertification monitoring faces limitations, including the lack of unified transboundary standards and the inability of single vegetation indices to comprehensively characterize the complex surface evolution of arid and semi-arid regions. To bridge this gap, a multidimensional desertification assessment framework that integrated machine learning methods and six monitoring indicators, including MSAVI, Albedo, TGSI, EVI, WET, and BSI, were introduced to analyze the spatiotemporal evolution of desertification across northern China and Mongolia from 2000 to 2024, and then the XGBoost–SHAP model was used to quantitatively identify the factors associated with desertification patterns. The main results are as follows: (1) The random forest method obtained the highest classification performance of the degree of desertification compared with the other methods, with an overall accuracy of 87.79% and a Kappa coefficient of 0.84. (2) From 2000 to 2024, desertification of the study area experienced a process of expansion, fluctuation, and reversal, with recovery being the dominant trend overall. Specifically, in northern China, the proportion of non-desertification consistently remained above 27%, while Mongolia was dominated by moderate and heavy desertification. (3) The area of severe desertification significantly decreased, and was largely converted to lighter desertification degrees. (4) Precipitation and livestock density emerged as key predictors of desertification patterns, with their relative contributions varying across ecological domains. The XGBoost–SHAP analysis further revealed nonlinear predictive associations and interactions among climatic and socioeconomic variables, highlighting the spatial heterogeneity of factors associated with desertification. The results provided scientific evidence and methodological support for the construction of the China–Mongolia–Russia Economic Corridor and the regional achievement of the land degradation neutrality (LDN) goal.

</details>
